# User Stories — Health by Dr Brad

The user-perspective spec for every journey in the tool. Written 2026-08-07, grounded in the first usage audit ([usage-audit-2026-08.md](usage-audit-2026-08.md)); the architecture reference is [architecture-v2.html](architecture-v2.html). Each story carries acceptance criteria (AC), the **usage evidence** we have, and the **test status** against the current suites.

## The product constitution (Brad, 2026-08-14 — read every story against this)

**What this is:** a preventative-care protocol generator plus a recall system. One five-minute check-up tells a user what to actually do — bloods, screenings, medications, supplements, weight, sleep, exercise — and the reminder calendar makes sure it keeps happening over the years. The website is the onboarding funnel and the calculator; **email is the product surface the user lives in.** For preventative care an annual touch is the correct cadence, not a retention failure — never chase app-engagement metrics here.

**The avatar:** 45–55, health-curious but unsystematic, low tech-literacy (they arrive from a longevity YouTube channel, so they already care; they just don't *systematize*). They will type an email address; they will mostly not connect a cloud drive — live funnel 2026-08-14: typed captures outnumber cloud connects roughly 10:1 on any denominator we have (~4–5 captures/day and 874+ lifetime vs 3 cloud connects EVER; a precise percentage depends on which visitor count you divide by, so the RATIO is the decision-driver). Design for zero thought: the right thing happens by default, visibly, with a one-click way out. The genuinely health-indifferent mass market only becomes reachable at the mobile app (US-25); don't contort the web product for a user who can't arrive yet.

**The promise (one sentence — keep it literally true):** *Your health data stays on your device. We keep one thing — your reminder calendar, what's due and when — so we can nudge you.* What may be STORED on Brad's server, visibly disclosed: reminder labels + due dates, the enrolled email, a capability token, and the date each label was last sent — that row and nothing else (`app/lib/reminder-v2.server.ts`). "No health data on the server" means no health VALUES; a screening label and a due date are not nothing, which is why they are disclosed at enrolment. What is never stored as health data: measurements, lab values, medications, results, reasoning. (Precision matters — the loop reads this literally: chat questions and lab-report uploads do TRANSIT the server for AI processing (US-12/US-15/US-16), in memory for extraction, and the blog chat bubble, Discord and YouTube persist their conversation logs in `chat_messages` — operational data the user typed at a chatbot, not a parsed health record. The main widget's chat, which sends the record with every turn, persists no message content at all since 2026-09-10 (US-15 AC7); the question text alone is kept 30 days for the matching audit and then removed. The promise is about what we KEEP as your health data: nothing.) A label can carry an inference (a lung-CT row implies smoking history); we **disclose that at enrolment rather than neuter the labels** — specific recall notices are what clinics already send, and specificity is what makes the email valuable standalone (Brad, 2026-08-14, superseding the "vague emails" option).

**Decisions, 2026-09-10 — every claim matches the code (Brad).** A member of the public can paste this repo into ChatGPT and ask it to find the theft. Brad did, on 2026-09-10: "no evidence of theft, but the claims are too broad." So the principle now applies to every privacy sentence on every surface — README, guides, consent screen, tool descriptions, the privacy addendum: say what the code does and no more. The hosted connector unseals the user's cloud credential and holds the record in server MEMORY for one call. An import writes nothing to the record until the user confirms; its candidates wait in a pending file in the user's own folder. The file's bytes go to Anthropic at the extract step, and Anthropic's retention is QUOTED and linked, never asserted by us. Reminders keep an email address and due dates, and "no health data" means no health VALUES. The GitHub Pages build makes two optional calls to Brad's server (Google token exchange, reminders) plus scrubbed Sentry. A claim we cannot point at code for gets narrowed, not softened. Where the day's decisions live: US-09 AC7 (Sentry free-text scrub), US-09 AC8 (Clarity replay masking), US-15 AC7 (the widget keeps no transcript, and the 266 old ones are deleted), US-18 AC5 (Klaviyo gets an address and nothing else), US-28 (the chat-digest email deleted), US-32 AC9/AC23 (`contact` refusals in `report_feedback`), US-35 AC7/AC13 (the stale sweep and the import wording), US-10 (the record-sync plan stopped). Two more, same day: `router_raw` and `router_error` join the 30-day chat purge, so no free text on a widget row outlives the question (US-15 AC7); and the google-drive reminders lane always returns its token, new row or existing, because that caller proved the inbox (US-17 AC8).

**Decisions, 2026-09-10 (afternoon): the second pass over the same principle.** The morning's audit fixed the claims; this pass fixes what the code does and what the docs still got wrong about it. Erase now reaches the health data outside the record file, in local-only mode too, and says what it cannot reach (US-11 AC1). Sentry's scrub redacts a family of credential keys, derives its vocabulary from the clinical catalogue instead of a retyped list, and is checked across the whole pipeline (US-09 AC7). `ChatEmbed` carries the Clarity mask itself, because it renders outside the widget root (US-09 AC8). `report_feedback` dedupes per connection, and its receipt echoes the report body, since the guard cannot read a diagnosis in prose (US-32 AC31/AC32). The lab-import poll is addressed by a token the caller holds (US-35 AC14). The blob-host regex is deleted rather than tuned (US-36 AC13). Four documentation corrections ride with them, each verified against the code: connecting a cloud on the storefront enrols you in reminders, so "if you enrol" was wrong; the blog bubble and the chatbot embed send the widget's cached inputs and keep their transcripts unpurged; `feedback_submissions` stores an address and up to 2000 characters of free text with no scrub and no retention limit; and the `report_feedback` guard's limits are stated where the user is asked to approve a public issue. That last question is now answered, in the US-15 header and AC8: the bubble keeps sending cached inputs, and its transcripts join the 30-day purge.

**Decisions, 2026-09-10 (evening): the third pass, on what the code still owed the promise.** The blog bubble and the chatbot embed keep sending the widget's cached values as context, and their transcripts join the 30-day purge instead of sitting unpurged (US-15). The ChatGPT file-link import route is deleted now rather than at the end of its 27 remaining days, so a dropped file reaches our server by no path at all (US-36). Erase tombstones the chat history and clears the local named files, and the confirm dialog names what erase cannot reach (US-11). Erase also writes a cancelled opt-in, so auto-enrolment cannot restart reminders after it (US-17). A reminder tombstone is deleted once it is 90 days old (US-17). A missing `cron_lock` row throws to Sentry instead of quietly consuming the day (US-17). `report_feedback` refuses file names, which carried more about a person than the report needed (US-32). Confirm-receipt replay across two machines stays an accepted residual, written down rather than fixed (US-32). `scopes_supported` is removed from the OAuth metadata because the server never honoured a scope, and metadata that promises one is a claim the code cannot back (US-09). (Reversed 2026-10-03, US-32 AC43: OpenAI's plugin dashboard offers no OAuth without advertised scopes, so the metadata now lists the pair every token already states, still unenforced.)

**Decisions, 2026-09-10 (night): the fourth pass, from an outside security read of the whole repo.** Nine things changed, eight with a test (the Discord change is read-verified only). A health file cannot ride into the server image: `.dockerignore` now mirrors every health-data pattern in `.gitignore` and a test fails when the two drift (US-09 AC9). `get-plan --html` writes the report `0600`, because it carries the same values as the record (US-33 AC5). The consent screen says what we can vouch for about the app asking — known client, registered at a named host, or nothing but the place it returns you — and refuses to be framed, since `frame-ancestors` is not covered by `default-src` (US-32 AC33). `/api/ab` gains a per-IP limiter, because the visitor id it was limited on rides in the body where a script can rotate it, and it checks the variant id, which no foreign key held (US-09 AC11). Feedback charges its 3/hour budget before the send rather than after it, so a Resend outage no longer hands out unlimited inserts, and the honeypot's unreachable second check is deleted rather than kept as reassurance (US-19 AC1/AC2). The Pages build stops rendering a feedback form that posts to an endpoint it does not have, and `sendFeedback` and the A/B calls take the surface guard `trackProductEvent` already had; the plan footer links the privacy notice on both builds, which until now was reachable only from the consent screen (US-09 AC10). The Discord bot stops attaching an author's Discord id and tag to Sentry diagnostics (US-09 AC6).

Six more were read and reported to Brad, and are NOT changed, because each is his call rather than a defect: the auto-ship human gate on workflow dispatch; a Content-Security-Policy for the GitHub Pages build; the Discord and YouTube transcript retention, which the evening pass left in place by decision; the membership oracle, accepted as a residual; the Google token exchange that routes through our server on the Pages build, which is disclosed in the README rather than removed; and an injection path in the YouTube bot. They are written here so the next pass finds them named rather than re-discovering them. (Two of these were reversed later that evening: Discord transcripts now join the 30-day purge, see US-15 AC8, and react-router was upgraded to 7.18.3, see the US-09 decision line.)

**Decisions, 2026-09-10 (late): the fifth pass, and the round that found a leak of ours.** `tools/output/youtube-dryrun-tR0ueKzXmZ8.html` was swept into a commit on 2026-08-10 carrying 14 YouTube commenters' handles beside their own health text, and it stayed public on GitHub for a month (US-09). The rest of the pass: what the host logs is stated as the path AND its query string, and no secret or health-bearing parameter travels in one any more, because the lab-import poll moved to a POST body (US-35 AC15, and "What we log" in the privacy addendum). The CLI's file adapter gets the treatment `get-plan --html` got: backups below the erase epoch removed, an exclusive random temp name that is fsynced, colon-free backup names, `0600` on the record, and both ignore files covering `health-roadmap.json.*` (US-31 AC8). Provider errors on the email lanes are logged without the recipient (US-22 AC11). The admin and ops test routes moved their side effects from GET loaders to POST actions behind an `ADMIN_SHOP_DOMAIN` gate (US-09 AC12). Sentry breadcrumb `data` became an allow-list rather than a scrub (US-09 AC7). Two residuals are written down rather than fixed: Shopify signs only the query string, so a captured signed URL replays with any body for ten minutes, and the Resend webhook has no replay nonce because its handler is idempotent (US-09). What the record file promises is now said in the user's own words on three surfaces: two devices apart converge to the newer value for a day with the other kept as `entered-in-error`, and the file is the trust root, so anything that can write it can already delete it and the provider's version history is the recovery (README, `getting-started.md`, the addendum). `recommendationSnapshots` turns out to be read by nobody, so the merge rule that drops the less rich of two loses nothing a user sees, and the field is a deletion candidate (US-10).

**Five for Brad from the same pass, each his call rather than a defect.** (1) The react-router 7.17.0 upgrade: three applicable advisories, 7.18.3 still needs the `dist/development` workaround, deferred to a canary deploy (US-09). (2) The Discord retention argument: those transcripts sit on a gated server, which is the argument for leaving them, and the evening pass already left them by decision. (3) Sentry on the GitHub Pages build is on by default rather than opt-in, and the build is served from `drbradstanfield.github.io`, a subdomain of the one registrable domain every GitHub Pages site sits under; everything under that subdomain shares one origin, which is why the README names what that origin's localStorage holds. (4) The YouTube bot sends commenters' display names to the model. (5) Rewriting git history to remove the leaked file, and paraphrase-on-ingest for the chat-health charter's rule of copying production failures verbatim into `tools/test-queries.json` (US-09). (Two of these were reversed later that evening: Discord transcripts now join the 30-day purge, see US-15 AC8, and react-router was upgraded to 7.18.3, see the US-09 decision line.)

**Decision, 2026-09-11 (Brad).** The connector, CLI and MCP layer stays open source and stays unmarketed: one link in the tool, "Supercharge this tool", is its only entry point, and it opens the hub guide (US-38). The product goal is health with as little thought as possible, and the avatar above will not connect a drive, let alone a terminal; the connectors serve the few who want them and must not add a step for everyone else.

**Additive promise — applies ONLY to users who connect an AI assistant (US-32 Phase 1).** The sentence above is untouched and stays literally true for everyone who never connects one. For those who do, a second sentence applies. It takes effect when Phase 1 is DEPLOYED; the code ships in this commit, the secrets and the DNS do not.

> *"If you connect an AI assistant, your record still lives only in your storage — but our server reads it, in memory, to answer your assistant, and your assistant holds a sealed credential only we can open. We keep no copy. You cancel it in your Dropbox or Google settings; that also disconnects this website from your folder, and you can reconnect here in one click."*

One exception, for one invented account (US-32 AC38, Brad 2026-10-02): while an OpenAI app review is pending, our server holds the Dropbox credential of the synthetic reviewer account, so OpenAI's reviewer can sign in on our consent page without a Dropbox login. That account belongs to nobody; every real user's credential stays sealed in their assistant's token as above.

**Durability (why localStorage loss is a bounded cost, not a crisis):** the artifacts survive — the PDF, every reminder email (each carries the full schedule per US-23 AC3 — for a typed user that's the newest copy we were given at capture, not a live sync), and calendar entries (US-24). Re-entry is cheap by design: the check-up takes five minutes, and re-entering IS re-assessing. Cloud sync (US-09) is the durability upgrade for the motivated, not the front door. The real fix is the mobile app (US-25). We never "fix" durability by holding health data server-side.

**Email architecture (decided 2026-08-11, reaffirmed 2026-08-14):** Resend sends everything programmatic — reminders, the plan-ready email, the feedback path. Klaviyo holds only the marketing list. Never merge them: Klaviyo suppression is list-wide, so a supplement-promo unsubscribe would silently kill "your colonoscopy is due" with no error anywhere. Two consents, two systems, two suppression lists.

**Architectural destination:** the mobile app (US-25) — on-device notifications (reminders with no server at all), HealthKit ingestion (data entry with no typing), durable storage (no wipe anxiety). Check near-term decisions against one question: *does this move toward or away from the phone?*

**Maintenance contract:** when a feature's behavior changes, update its story here in the same commit, then regenerate the browser companion: `npx tsx scripts/build-user-stories-html.ts` → `docs/user-stories.html` (linked from architecture-v2.html; never edit the html by hand). New tests cite the story ID (e.g. `US-04`) in a comment. New features follow CLAUDE.md → Development Pathway Lane B: story + acceptance criteria + a declared usage signal BEFORE code. The weekly product-health loop reads this file to judge whether reported friction contradicts an AC.

Legend for test status: ✅ covered · 🟡 logic covered but UI/journey untested · ❌ untested.

---

## Epic A — Entering health data

### US-01 · Progressive first fill (guest)
As a first-time visitor, I can start getting value by entering just sex + height, and the form progressively reveals more fields so I'm never confronted with a wall of inputs.
- AC1: Stage 1 shows only Units/Sex/Height; filling Sex+Height reveals Stage 2 (weight, waist, BP, birth month); filling Weight reveals Stage 3 (bloods, meds, screening, supplements).
- AC2: The next-expected field pulses (`.field-attention`).
- AC3: A returning visitor with saved data sees the full form immediately.
- AC4: Everything works with zero account/cloud — data persists in localStorage. Components import the local-first data module directly; retired account flags cannot enable an old server health-data path.
- Evidence: recordings show fast Stage-1 completion (8 clicks in 72s) and long engaged fills; blog→"Build my free plan"→tool is the working funnel.
- Tests: 🟡 `computeFormStage` in mappings.test.ts; no test that stages actually gate rendering. Gap: none planned (low churn).

### US-02 · Vitals entry incl. blood-pressure pair
As a user, I can type height, weight, waist and a paired systolic/diastolic BP quickly, and **my first click/tap on any cell or its unit label focuses the input** — no dead clicks.
- AC1: Clicking anywhere in a vitals cell (including placeholder text and the `mmHg`/unit chip) focuses the editable input.
- AC2: A BP entry is accepted only as a complete sys/dia pair; a half-pair doesn't save.
- AC3: Values are validated against clinical ranges with ok/warn/bad ticks before save.
- AC4: Numeric fields accept only numeric characters — letters and symbols are blocked at the keystroke (and stripped on paste), on every surface incl. Safari where `type="number"` doesn't enforce this.
- AC5: An out-of-range value shows a visible inline error (e.g. "Max 250 mmHg") on the field itself — never a silent save-block where garbage looks accepted.
- AC6: After typing an unambiguous, in-range systolic value, focus auto-advances to diastolic (immediately for 3-digit values 100–250; after a short pause for 2-digit values 60–99 that could still gain a digit).
- Evidence: **top friction in the audit** — dead clicks on the BP `▫▫▫/? mmHg` control and `mmHg` label in 3 of 15 recorded sessions; tooltip/units-picker dead clicks in 4.
- Tests: 🟡 `StartingInfoVitals.test.ts` covers BP pair routing/readiness logic; `blood-test-cell.test.ts` covers validation. ❌ No test that a cell-shell click focuses the input (the actual reported friction). **Action: fix + test (2026-08 backlog #1).**

### US-03 · Blood-test matrix entry & backfill
As a user, I can enter today's blood results in a draft column, and backfill older results under their historical date, and the matrix keeps every column aligned and scroll-synced.
- AC1: Draft column accepts values + a date (DraftDateCell); saving appends immutable measurements under that date. Its header reads "New" above the date, so it is never a second copy of a saved column's header (2026-09-22: the draft column and today's saved column both read "23 Sep"). The device keeps the date only when the user picked it: an unpicked New column is today on every load. A date cleared in the picker (iOS Reset) is never taken; the column keeps its day (2026-09-24: a draft left overnight dated the next morning's values to the day before, and a cleared date was written as an empty one).
- AC2: Typing into an existing dated column routes as backfill for that date. A value typed into an empty cell that is filled elsewhere before it is committed (another device, a connector) is off screen from then on, and is never committed over the new value (2026-09-24). Every cell's write names the row it expects to replace: the one the page showed under its slot when the user began typing there, or none. The store's own rows decide as the write lands: a slot holding any other row by then writes nothing of the cell, which keeps its value with the error "This test already has a value on that day in another column." (2026-09-24, round 3: a New value corrected a connector's value unseen; round 5, Codex: the check read the page, which shows an older record than the store while a profile edit holds a re-read back, so it happened again with no error shown). Typing again never clears that error; emptying the cell does, and what is typed next expects what the page shows then. A blood pressure is one reading: its halves are one cell, which expects the rows under both, so a half filled elsewhere refuses the pair. A saved column holding one half shows it ("130/—") and offers nothing to type over it; the other half goes in through the New column, dated that day (round 4: a systolic a connector added was corrected with a typed one, unseen; round 5: such a column showed an empty pair).
- AC3: At most one active row per (metric, day); a same-day re-entry routes through correction (US-04), never a silent overwrite or lost draft. This holds on every entry surface, the first-time vitals fields included: on 2026-09-22 a same-day fix to a weight was answered as a "duplicate", counted as saved and cleared. HealthTool now routes every save, from the fields and from both matrices, against the store's own rows (`routeTasksToSaves`). A value that changes nothing writes nothing: the number shown, typed again in any spelling, or the stored value itself, asked in the unit the number was typed in, whatever the display shows when it is committed (round 5, Codex: 185 lb typed over today's 84 kg, then switched back to kg, was saved as a correction to 83.9 kg; round 7: the first-time fields did the same, and now keep the unit a weight or waist is typed in, in the field or to the chat, while the form holds that value). The chat's pre-fill of the vitals matrix is typed as the chat's number, in the unit it was stated (2026-09-25: it was typed converted to the cell's unit and rounded to the display, so "185 lb" over today's 84 kg, shown in kg, was saved as a correction to 83.9 kg). The blood-test matrix's pre-fill still converts into the cell's unit and rounds to the display: that matrix keeps no unit per cell, and whether it should is Brad's call. The number typed is never rounded to decide that, so 3.24 over a shown 3.2 corrects it. The field says what a save will do: "Replaces 82 kg" when today already holds a value. A save takes out of the form only the value it saved: a number typed into the field while its save runs stays, and the next save saves it (2026-09-25: the save took the field out by name, the newer number with it). Only the fields' own save takes a value out; a matrix's save of the same number, for another day, leaves it. A value typed into a first-time field and not yet left, when a phone turned across 768 px builds the form again with the vitals as a matrix, is on screen nowhere, but the next save of the fields (Save as PDF, or an upload's start) writes it under today: the user typed it, and dropping it would lose it silently (2026-09-25; until then it was never written). Like every first-time value, it names the row it replaces only as it saves, so if today's slot holds a value by then, that save corrects it (still open). Two typed cells for one test on one day (the draft dated onto a saved column, and that column's empty cell) are an error on both, "This test already has a value on that day in another column.", never a silent pick: neither is saved until the user clears one, and the rest of the draft saves (2026-09-24: both went to insert, the store refused the second and the page reported success; a first fix saved the one typed last).
- AC4: A failed save preserves the typed draft for retry, and the matrix says "Some values did not save. Try again." HealthTool reads every write's answer, slot by slot, so a refused write is never counted as saved: what landed leaves the draft and the form, and only a refused cell stays, with the record's reason on it (2026-09-24, round 3: a commit that partly landed kept every cell, and the next leave wrote a saved value again over the user's correction of it).
- AC5 (2026-09-22; history in the reference.md gotcha archive): what is typed into either matrix is a draft, kept on the device exactly as typed and saved exactly as typed. A vitals unit switch shows each typed weight or waist, draft and backfills alike, in the new unit as the same quantity, and the commit is the number typed, never the rounded display (2026-09-24: 84 kg through lb and back saved 83.9, and an 80 kg backfill switched to lb saved 80 lb). The draft is committed only when the user leaves the matrix (a tap or a focus outside it; a touch that becomes a scroll is not a tap), presses Enter, taps the blood-test matrix's tick or starts a lab upload, which commits both matrices before any file is read; a commit saves the valid cells and leaves any other in the draft with its error. Hiding or closing the page keeps the draft on the device and writes nothing to the record.
- AC6 (2026-09-24; its re-read rule decided again by Brad on 2026-09-25): a draft value typed on this load of the page stands in for the record in the plan (and so the PDF) and the chat while it could be saved, clashes with no other cell, its slot still holds the rows it expected, and it is its test's latest. One that clashes, whose slot another writer has filled, or dated before the record's own latest value for that test, leaves them to the record (it drove both). A blood pressure is the exception to "could be saved": each half stands in on its own, in range, though only the pair can be saved, so a systolic typed alone puts the new systolic beside the record's older diastolic in the plan and the chat. Owed to the journey redesign: lend a half only with its pair. A draft restored from the device does not stand in until it is typed into on this load of the page; whether it should is Brad's call. A matrix leaving the page takes back what it lent, and one built again on the same page (a phone turned across 768 px) lends again what was typed on it (round 4: after a phone turned, emptying or back-dating the cell left the old number in the plan, the chat and the PDF; round 5: a blood-test draft restored on opening stood in at once). A remote change or an upload does not end a draft's standing in, and nothing need be typed again. Brad decided this on 2026-09-25: the draft is still on screen, and the checks above judge it again on every render, against the record just read. So it stops standing in only when the new record fills its slot or holds a later value for its test. Round 7 had a re-read hand the plan and the chat back to the record until the draft was typed into again (Codex found LDL "3" typed on to "3.0" left the plan on the record). That rule made every path that replaced the form tell the matrices, and it was patched three times in two days. What the drafts lend now lives apart from the form, and only the matrices write it (HealthTool's `lent`), so a re-read cannot drop it and each matrix knows what it lent.
- Evidence: engaged sessions show field-by-field entry of masked numeric values; feedback email 2026-03-22 reported values appearing under a wrong date (root cause never found; the save that AC5 replaced is one cause, reproduced live on 2026-09-23 from the 2026-09-22 feedback). Corrections (product-health): `correction_made` 6 → 25 in 2026-W39 from 25 distinct visitors — 19 before the fix deployed (edu-89, ~09-24T22Z; 7 on the 09-20 reach spike) and 6 after; fcea2ee moved the emit to HealthTool.tsx and it now also counts a Replace, so the series definition changed on 09-24. The 2026-09-22 feedback was answered by code within three days.
- Tests: ✅ `matrix-save.test.ts` (task routing; a re-entry that changes nothing writes nothing, 3.24 over 3.2 corrects), `blood-test-cell.test.ts` (validation; `sameAsSaved`), merge tests (slot invariant), and date-defaulting semantics pinned in `roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts` (2026-08-07). ✅ Real WebKit, 2026-09-25 (iPhone 13 emulation, the live page with the local build swapped in, every POST and tracker blocked): AC3's unit switch in the first-time fields writes nothing 63 ms before the save and a new number in lb corrects once, unrounded; AC6's re-read across two tabs hands the plan back to the record and typing "3.0" lends the draft again (superseded by the 2026-09-25 decision: a re-read now leaves a draft standing in); the pre-fix bundle fails both. The 24 checks from 2026-09-24 still pass. Live on health-roadmap-edu-89, where `tools/webkit-verify.mjs` passed on desktop and iPhone. ✅ 2026-09-24: `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (the "New" header; moving between cells never commits; a press, a tap on nothing focusable or a focus outside commits once; a touch outside that turns into a scroll never commits; a value typed into the cell that kept the focus commits on the next leave; Enter and the tick; the date picker opened by a press and from the keyboard, committed under the picked date only on leaving; two cells for one slot an error on both, neither sent until one is cleared; a cell the test cannot take kept with its error while the rest is sent; the page hiding commits nothing and the draft comes back after a reload; the draft leaving the device only once the save is accepted; a cell filled elsewhere since never committed; a failed save keeping the draft and saying so), `StartingInfoVitals.test.ts` (`vitalsCellsOf`: what a vitals column commits, as typed) and `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (over a real store: the date picked after the value; the vitals matrix committing only on leaving; the page hiding mid-entry writing nothing, on localStorage and on a cloud whose upload never lands, the drafts back after a reload; two cells for one slot in both matrices; the vitals draft keeping what was typed; a unit switch re-expressing a typed weight; a backfill whose cell a connector filled meanwhile never committed over it; leftover form state never writing a value: a corrected waist survives Save as PDF, a draft dated 20 Aug never rewrites today; the same-day weight fix and its "Replaces" line). `matrix-save.test.ts`: one write per slot as a safety net, a refused insert fails the save. ✅ 2026-09-24, round 3: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (a unit switch re-expressing every typed vitals cell, draft and backfill, saved exactly as typed; a lab upload committing a vitals draft restored from the device; a New value whose slot another writer filled since it was typed an error, and a correction once typed again; an unpicked New column today after a night; a cleared date never taken; a commit that partly lands keeping only the refused cell, with its reason; clashing or back-dated draft values leaving the plan and the chat to the record), `matrix-save.test.ts` (refusals answered slot by slot) and `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (a draft value whose slot was filled since it was typed). ✅ 2026-09-24, round 4: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (a BP typed into a saved column's empty cell, either half filled elsewhere after the pair was typed or between its halves: nothing written, the half shown; a draft value, the phone turned and back, then emptied or back-dated: the plan, the chat and the PDF read the record). ✅ 2026-09-24, round 5: `matrix-save.test.ts` (a cell whose slot holds another row than it names writes nothing and answers `changed`, a blood pressure as a pair; "changes nothing" asked in the unit the cell carries), `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (a draft value whose slot was filled since it was typed names the empty slot until it is emptied; a refused cell off the screen no longer holds up "did not save") and `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (with a profile edit holding a re-read back: a New value, a value emptied and typed again, and a blood pressure, each whose slot another writer filled, refused as written; typing again never clears the error; a column holding half a blood pressure shows it; 84 kg re-entered as 185 lb and switched back to kg writes nothing, 190 lb corrects it exactly; a matrix leaving the form takes back what it lent; restored drafts, blood and vitals, wait until typed into, and a phone turned keeps what was typed standing in). ✅ 2026-09-25, round 7: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (in the first-time fields, today's 84 kg re-entered as 185 lb and switched back to kg before the save writes nothing, 190 lb corrects it exactly, the chat's 185 lb over it, shown in kg, writes nothing, and a blood pressure saves as a pair and, typed again across a unit switch, writes nothing; after a remote change, the same number typed again into a draft stands in again; after an upload, a phone turned lends a vitals draft nothing until it is typed into, and lends it once it is; these two superseded by the 2026-09-25 decision). ✅ 2026-09-25, cleanup: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (a weight typed into its first-time field while that field's save runs stays in the form and is saved next; the chat's 185 lb over today's 84 kg in the vitals matrix, shown in kg, writes nothing, and 190 lb corrects it exactly) and `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (a re-read takes back what the draft lent with nothing above the matrix rendering, and typing lends it again; superseded by the 2026-09-25 decision). ✅ 2026-09-25, AC6 decided again: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (across a remote change and across an upload, a draft typed on this page stands in with nothing typed again, and "3" typed on to "3.0" changes nothing; across an upload, a vitals draft stands in and a phone turned keeps it standing in; after a remote change, a draft whose slot another writer filled, or that is no longer its test's latest, leaves the plan and the chat to the record while another draft still stands in; half a blood pressure in the vitals matrix stands in, and the fields' save as an upload starts writes nothing) and `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (a new history judges the draft again with nothing typed). These replace round 7's two AC6 tests and the cleanup's re-read test. Round 7's real-WebKit check of AC6 verified the old rule; the new one is not yet checked in real WebKit. ✅ 2026-09-25, AC3: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (a first-time waist typed and not left, then the phone turned so the form is built again with the vitals as a matrix, is written under today by Save as PDF; a first-time weight survives the vitals matrix saving the same number for another day, and an upload's start writes it). ❌ The native iOS date picker and a real iPhone's presses are hand-tested only. ✅ Real WebKit, 2026-09-25, AC6 as Brad decided it (same harness, 650c39f): after the page takes in another tab's write, a draft LDL typed on the page still stands in (the plan keeps 3.0) with nothing retyped and nothing saved; once another writer fills the draft's slot, the plan shows the record and the cell says the day already has a value. The pre-fix build shows the record after the re-read. Live on health-roadmap-edu-90.

### US-04 · Correcting a saved value (FHIR)
As a user who mistyped or whose lab import was wrong, I can click the saved cell, type the right value, and the display updates — while the old value is preserved as `entered-in-error` with a `correctsId` chain (never mutated or deleted).
- AC1: Correction happens on the surface where I see the value (matrix cell), not a separate admin page. An emptied editor closes and writes nothing (2026-09-22: it stayed open with nothing in it), and a value that changes nothing writes nothing (US-03 AC3). An editor left open while its value is replaced (another device, a connector) writes nothing over the new one: it closes and the cell says "This value changed on another device, so your edit was not saved." (2026-09-24: it corrected the new row back to the old number, core and lab values alike).
- AC2: Old row flips to `entered-in-error`; new row has `source='manual_correction'`, `correctsId=old id`; results use only active rows.
- AC3: Corrections converge across devices (status is sticky in merge).
- AC4: Correcting a non-latest value doesn't disturb the latest summary.
- AC5 (proposed 2026-09-28, Brad: "probably"; for the blood-test redesign, not built): A correction can move a value to the day it was really measured. The wrong-day row flips to `entered-in-error` and a new row on the right day carries `correctsId`, so the history keeps both. A blood test moves as a whole, since its values share one draw date. If the new day already holds an active value for that test, the person chooses which one is right; a day never keeps two. Why: the plan reads the latest value, so an old result filed under today drives today's plan, and nothing can fix it now.
- Evidence: correction affordance was a deliberate UX decision (memory: corrections live with the data); `correction_made` funnel event now measures real usage — 9 corrections in the first instrumented week (2026-W32).
- Tests: ✅ merge.test.ts (`correctsId`/sticky status) + `roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts` (store-level correction flow, 2026-08-07; `correctValue` on core and lab rows through `record-edits.correctValue`, and its range refusal, 2026-09-24). ✅ 2026-09-24: `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (the empty editor closes, the same number writes nothing, a refusal keeps the editor open) and `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (a correction reaches the plan and the chat context, also after two tests were saved together and when a connector made it: the page re-reads the record rather than patching its copy, and a save leaves no copy of what it saved in the form; an editor over a value corrected elsewhere, core or lab, writes nothing and says so). ✅ Round 5: `BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx` (the editor's correction names the row it opened on, which the store refuses once replaced; that refusal closes the editor and says so).

### US-05 · Unit switching
As a US/international user, I can flip between conventional and SI units at any time; every field, threshold, and suggestion re-renders correctly and stored data never changes (SI canonical at rest).
- Tests: ✅ units.test.ts round-trips + thresholds; validation error conversion covered. UI re-render untested (accepted).

### US-06 · Medications, supplements & screenings
As a user, I record what I actually take (real drug + dose, or none/not-tolerated/not-yet) and my screening dates; the plan's medication cascade and screening nudges follow the algorithm doc.
- AC1: FHIR MedicationStatement semantics per CLAUDE.md table; history is append-only change log.
- AC2: Brad's products appear as quick-add supplement chips.
- AC3: New screening types must round-trip through the file (7-step checklist in CLAUDE.md).
- AC4: History charts show recorded medication starts, stops, switches and dose changes. Dates mean when the change was recorded, not a claimed treatment start date. Legacy rows without a change type stay stored but produce no invented event. Stops with a status placeholder identify the medication category. Chart ranges include recorded events even with one old result; each pin stays at its exact recorded date, with a numbered dated list below. Selecting or keyboard-focusing a list entry shows only that event's number, above other lines, using its stable row ID; dense same-time events remain readable.
- AC5: The form never recommends, targets or steps through what the plan does not (Brad, 2026-09-25; widened to every section 2026-09-26). HealthTool builds one `planBasis` from the validated inputs and results the plan used: the weight trigger (`weightMedicationTrigger`), the plan's lipid marker, age, BMI, ideal body weight and waist target. The form reads that, never raw typed values:
  - the two medication sections, including their step disclosure (`weightCascade` / `lipidCascade` in health-core's medication-cascades.ts, the functions the plan's cards come from);
  - the blood-pressure target hint;
  - the vitals matrix targets and status colours;
  - the screening and bone-density sections;
  - the upload review's screening eligibility.

  A cascade's "may benefit from medications" sentence shows only while the plan shows one of its step cards. With every step answered, the section goes neutral (adversarial review, 2026-09-25: BMI 32 and an HbA1c of 40 with every step not tolerated recommended in the form alone). With no plan yet (no valid sex or height), everything stays neutral, blood pressure included. Cases that used to differ:
  - total cholesterol 3.84 with HDL 2.20 (the form rounded non-HDL its own way; both now read the plan's exact 1.64, at or above the 1.6 target);
  - an LDL above the validation bound (the plan sets it aside);
  - born 1958 with no birth month and a BP of 135/85 (the target is <130/80);
  - a GLP-1 or statin with no dose recorded (the plan moved to the next step; since AC8 the plan and the form both ask for the dose);
  - HDL at or above total cholesterol (the plan falls back to LDL).
- AC6 (Brad, 2026-09-26 and 2026-09-28): the weight and diabetes medication cascade turns on:
  - at a BMI of 30 or more;
  - above 28, unless the waist is healthy (waist-to-height under 0.5) and no marker is raised;
  - from 25 with any raised marker, even when the waist is healthy. Under 25 it stays off.

  The raised markers are:
  - HbA1c 38.8 mmol/mol (5.7%) or more;
  - triglycerides 150 mg/dL (about 1.7 mmol/L) or more;
  - stage 1 blood pressure (systolic 130 or more, or diastolic over 80: the blood-pressure card's own test);
  - waist-to-height 0.5 or more;
  - the plan's cholesterol marker (ApoB, else non-HDL, else LDL) at the 2018 AHA/ACC risk-enhancing lines: ApoB 1.3 g/L, non-HDL 190 mg/dL (4.913 mmol/L), LDL 160 mg/dL (4.138 mmol/L).

  There is no waiting period: the cards suggest the medications alongside diet, exercise and sleep. These thresholds are deliberately more proactive than the guidelines (Brad: "I want to be a bit more proactive compared to what the guidelines suggest"). roadmap_text.html says so, and says some people it flags fall below the licensed thresholds. One function drives the step cards, the standalone card, the blood-pressure card's GLP-1 line and the form. Non-HDL is compared unrounded (a rounded 1.6 once flagged a real 1.56, and 190 mg/dL never reached its line); BMI and waist-to-height, which are unitless, keep their rounded rule.
- AC7 (2026-09-28, adversarial review): changing one medication or its dose never records a change to another, and a step that holds a recorded value always shows. Before, changing a GLP-1 dose in the cascade wrote "not taking" rows for an SGLT2 inhibitor and metformin the person still took, and those rows are permanent. A drug or dose change now resets only that drug's own escalation answer. A dose that was never recorded reads "Add dose" (AC8 renamed it from "Dose not recorded", which a 390 px phone cut to "Dose not re"), and an answer the form has no option for (a raw 'ezetimibe' row, an agent's 'yes') shows as recorded, not as the first option. **Round 3 (2026-09-28):** the escalation reset stays in cascade mode only, where the question is on screen. A flat-mode reset was tried and reverted: it silently changed an answer the person could not see, and every change writes a permanent row. Residual: a dose changed in flat mode keeps its earlier escalation answer. Also, a DEXA follow-up marked scheduled now offers future dates, as the cancer screenings do. An empty drug name reads "Not recorded", not "Other (recorded)", since the plan reads it as unanswered. A name that is not a string (null, a number or an object in a hand-edited file) is skipped by `medicationsToInputs`, as if no row were recorded, so the plan, the form and the chat all read it as unanswered; a number there used to crash the page (`statinDrug.charAt` in suggestions.ts). Tests: `HealthTool.med-changes.test.tsx`, `mappings.test.ts`, `chat-context.test.ts`.
- AC8 (Brad, 2026-09-28): a listed GLP-1 or statin with no dose recorded asks for the dose, since the next step depends on it. Before, the plan skipped "increase your dose" and moved to the next drug.
  - Weight: on a listed GLP-1 (tirzepatide, semaglutide injection or oral, dulaglutide) with no dose, the plan shows only `weight-med-glp1-dose` ("Add your GLP-1 dose"), whatever the escalation answer, and the later steps stay closed.
  - Cholesterol: on a listed statin with no dose, once ezetimibe is answered, `med-statin-dose` ("Add your statin dose") takes the slot of the step up or the PCSK9 inhibitor, whatever those answers say. Bempedoic acid still shows first while unanswered.
  - The form, in cascade mode, puts "Add your dose: your next step depends on it." in the drug's hint line, under its label, while the plan asks, and the empty dose box reads "Add dose". Choosing a dose opens the next step, and the drug's description comes back. A panel too narrow for the drug name and its dose puts the dose on its own line, so neither is cut off.
  - Each card names only the steps its drug can take next (adversarial review, 2026-09-28): "A higher dose or a switch to Tirzepatide may help" and "A higher dose or a more potent statin may help"; on tirzepatide and rosuvastatin, whose highest doses move on to the next medication, "A higher dose may help, or you may already take the highest."
  - Adding the dose resets that drug's escalation answer, as any dose change does (AC7), so the question is asked again. Keeping the answer was tried and reverted (adversarial review, 2026-09-28): a chat edit can switch the drug without a dose, so the answer on record may belong to another drug, and keeping it would skip that drug's step up. Re-answering costs one click, and since AC11 a reset records no history event.
  - Each card's "Why this suggestion?" text is drug-neutral: "Which step comes next depends on the dose you take now."
  - Unchanged: "Other GLP-1" (still the switch step), not tolerated, "haven't tried yet", an unlisted name such as liraglutide (the form cannot give it a dose), a statin before ezetimibe is answered, and flat mode.
  - A dose the form does not list is graded by AC10.
- AC9 (Brad, 2026-09-28): with every cholesterol step answered and the marker still at or above its target, the plan shows no further card. Brad: "no, because there's nothing else that clinically can be done if taking statin, ezetimibe, and PCSK9i". Today's behaviour is kept, and the algorithm doc records the decision.
- AC10 (Brad, 2026-09-28): a dose the form does not list is graded as that drug's nearest listed dose. On a tie it takes the lower dose, so a step up is never skipped.
  - Before, such a dose was graded as the drug's highest. The chat's medication tool accepts any positive dose, so this happened in practice. Rosuvastatin 2.5 mg and pravastatin 10 mg went straight to the PCSK9 inhibitor. Semaglutide injection 2.0 mg went to the switch to tirzepatide.
  - Examples now:

    | Recorded dose | Graded as | Plan suggests |
    |---|---|---|
    | Rosuvastatin 2.5 mg | 5 mg | Increase the dose |
    | Pravastatin 10 mg | 20 mg | Increase the dose |
    | Atorvastatin 30 mg (a tie) | 20 mg | Increase the dose |
    | Simvastatin 80 mg | 40 mg, its highest | Switch to a more potent statin |
    | Semaglutide injection 2.0 mg | 1.7 mg | Increase the dose |
    | Tirzepatide 3 mg | 2.5 mg | Increase the dose |
  - The statin's potency is read at the graded dose, and the switch card says "at or near the maximum dose".
  - evidence.ts is unchanged: the rule maps a recorded dose onto the existing dose lists and changes no reason, guideline tag or citation.
  - The form still shows the recorded dose as "Other (recorded)".
  - Unchanged: a listed dose, a missing dose (AC8), and a drug the form does not list (liraglutide).
- AC11 (from the Codex review, 2026-09-28; Brad: "fix"): an escalation answer is not a medication.
  - Saving `statin_escalation` or `glp1_escalation` records no start, stop or change in the medication history, whatever the value.
  - A row an earlier build wrote for one is not shown: not as a chart pin, and not in the history view's list or its toggle. The file keeps it (append-only).
  - An escalation answer is not an active medication, so on its own it schedules no medication review (`isActiveMedication`; /simplify altitude review, 2026-09-28).
  - One helper, `isEscalationKey`, holds the rule for the history writer, the history view and the reminders. The hosted connector's `read_record` still returns the file whole, as its contract says.
  - Why: an answer of "yes" (which only an agent or the chat writes) counted as taking. Its first save recorded a start, and any reset to "not yet" recorded a stop, drawn as "Recorded stop: Glp1 escalation" on the HbA1c, weight and triglyceride charts.
- AC12 (from the adversarial review, 2026-09-28; Brad: "fix"): a chat edit that changes the statin or the GLP-1, the drug or its dose, resets that drug's escalation answer to "not yet", as the form's changes do (AC7).
  - Why: "I switched to atorvastatin" through the chat kept rosuvastatin's "didn't tolerate a higher dose", and the plan skipped increasing atorvastatin.
  - An answer the same reply states wins, whatever its order in the reply ("I switched to atorvastatin 40 and couldn't go higher").
  - The chat's Undo restores every medication row the reply changed, the answer included. A key that had no row goes back to its unanswered value ('none' for a drug, 'not_yet' for ezetimibe, the PCSK9 inhibitor, bempedoic acid and an escalation; `UNANSWERED_OF`), since a row is never removed and 'none' would read as an answer for those steps (Codex and adversarial review, 2026-09-28). The value leaves the medication steps where no row would, but a row is still not the same as none: any statin row switches the plan's cholesterol cascade on in place of the standalone cards, as the form's own "Haven't tried yet" does, and the form shows a step that holds a recorded value. Residual: a reply that changes a drug away and back resets its answer, which Undo restores. The reply's field edits are drafts the person saves or discards, so Undo leaves them.
  - It applies whether or not the cascade is showing, because the chat changes the drug on purpose and the answer was about the old one. The form's flat mode still keeps the answer (AC7's residual).
  - Unchanged: an edit to any other medication, and one that changes neither the drug nor the dose.
- Usage signal: `medication_history_viewed` measures annotated-chart reach; no medication names, doses or dates enter telemetry. Evidence (product-health 2026-W38): 0 events all-time since AC4 shipped; the emit is in the deployed HistoryPanel chunk but gated on a chart carrying a post-AC4 medication change, so the zero is unverified — one deliberate production view settles it (W38 backlog #4). W39: still 0 (third carry).
- Tests: ✅ suggestions/cascade logic in health-core; ✅ roadmap-store covers med/supplement history appends and US-06 AC4 projection; medication-annotations tests cover labels/stops/legacy rows/dose edge cases; HistoryPanel.test.tsx covers numbered pins, list order, toggle and the event (live verification pending); screening round-trip tests exist in mappings.test.ts. ✅ AC5 weight section: HealthTool.weight-meds.test.tsx renders the widget over a real store and reads the plan and the form from one page (10 cases; 2026-09-25: every step answered at BMI 25.0 and at BMI 32 is neutral, a later step's card recommends); suggestions.test.ts pins the trigger to the plan's earlier decisions in a 23-row table (2026-09-25; live verification pending). ✅ AC5 every section (2026-09-28): the form reads `planBasis`, and HealthTool.weight-meds.test.tsx checks the plan and the form on one page for cholesterol (non-HDL around the target, LDL above the validation bound, HDL at or above total cholesterol), blood-pressure targets (birth year with no month; no plan), the matrix targets and the upload review's age. A golden fixture generated from the pre-refactor plan pins the medication cascades' card ids. ✅ AC6: suggestions.test.ts trigger table (42+ rows), card wording, and pipeline tests for non-HDL at its lines. ✅ AC7: dose and drug changes write only their own rows (live verification pending). ✅ AC8 (2026-09-28): medication-cascades.test.ts covers every listed GLP-1 and statin with no dose under each answer, and the unchanged cases; suggestions.test.ts pins both cards' text and evidence; HealthTool.weight-meds.test.tsx shows the dose card and the form's hint, the next step hidden, and a chosen dose opening it; the golden record changed only in the dose rows (live verification pending). AC9 is today's behaviour, pinned by HealthTool.weight-meds.test.tsx (every cholesterol step answered: no card). ✅ Real WebKit, 2026-09-25 (same harness): BMI 28.6 with a waist-to-height ratio of 0.47 showed the neutral text and no GLP-1 card; BMI 32 showed the recommending sentence beside the plan's GLP-1 card. The pre-change build recommended in the first case. Live on health-roadmap-edu-91. ✅ Real WebKit on the live page, 2026-09-28 (health-roadmap-edu-92, Fly v471/v108): BMI 26 with a waist-to-height ratio of 0.45 and an HbA1c of 40 showed the GLP-1 card "With a BMI of 26 and prediabetic HbA1c, you may benefit from discussing Tirzepatide (preferred) or Semaglutide with your doctor, alongside diet, exercise and sleep…" and the form's matching sentence (AC5, AC6); changing a GLP-1 dose with an SGLT2 inhibitor and metformin on record wrote one GLP-1 row and nothing else, and every select kept its recorded value (AC7). ✅ Real WebKit on the live page, 2026-09-28 (health-roadmap-edu-93, Fly v472/v109; every POST and tracker blocked), AC8:
  - Semaglutide injection with no dose, at BMI 32: the plan showed only "Add your GLP-1 dose" ("…A higher dose or a switch to Tirzepatide may help…"). The GLP-1 field's hint read "Add your dose: your next step depends on it.", the dose box read "Add dose", and there was no SGLT2 inhibitor field. Choosing 1 mg brought back the drug's description, "Tried increasing GLP-1 dose?" and the increase card.
  - Rosuvastatin with no dose plus ezetimibe, on an iPhone 13: the plan showed "Add your statin dose" ("…A higher dose may help, or you may already take the highest…") after the bempedoic acid card, and no PCSK9 inhibitor field.
  - Layout: the drug and dose selects stacked at full width (346 px on the iPhone 13, 405 px on desktop), with no horizontal scroll.
  - ✅ AC10-AC12 (2026-09-28):
    - AC10: suggestions.test.ts 'US-06 AC10: gradedDose and the dose helpers' covers ties, including decimal ones, both ends, prototype keys and unchanged listed doses, plus 'the statin switch card' ("at or near the maximum dose"). medication-cascades.test.ts has 13 cascade and plan rows. The golden record's full set gained two off-list doses below each drug's highest (a tie between the two lowest, and one nearer the top). Over the new set, every changed row is an off-list dose, 1,440 weight rows and 5,733 lipid rows, and each reads as HEAD read it at the graded dose (scratchpad proof, 2026-09-28).
    - AC11: history-change.test.ts (every escalation transition gives null), roadmap-store.test.ts (no history row; an old escalation row stays in the file but not in the history view), medication-annotations.test.ts (no pin for a stored escalation row) and reminder-schedule.test.ts (an escalation "yes" alone schedules no medication review). The last two were checked by mutation.

Atorvastatin 70 mg showed "You're at or near the maximum dose of Atorvastatin…". AC11 and AC12 are pinned by unit and widget tests; a live chat edit was not driven.

## Epic B — Getting the plan

### US-07 · Personalized suggestions with evidence
As a user, once I've entered data I see prioritized suggestions (urgent/attention/info), each expandable to its clinical reason with guideline tags and references I can verify.
- AC1: Suggestions follow `health_roadmap_algorithm.md`; evidence text from `evidence.ts`; `roadmap_text.html` stays consistent (three-file sync rule).
- AC2: LDL-only entry (no total cholesterol) still produces lipid advice (regression: feedback 2026-03-06, fixed).
- AC3: New/changed suggestions highlight briefly so I notice what my new data changed.
- AC4: Results and suggestions are computed from the most recent active value per metric by clinical date (`recordedAt`); backfilled older readings never drive the plan.
- AC5 (Brad, 2026-09-28: "optimal is < 1.4"): LDL and non-HDL count as optimal only below the plan's treatment targets, 1.4 and 1.6 mmol/L.
  - From there up to Borderline (130 and 160 mg/dL, 3.362 and 4.138 mmol/L), the tiles say "Above optimal".
  - Borderline, High and Very high are unchanged.
  - Amber starts at the target, as ApoB's does at 0.5 g/L, in both the tiles and the history matrix.
  - The borderline cards state the same optimal line.
  - The lipid-diet advice starts there too.
  - Each "<X" prints the first value, at the display step, that the plan grades at or above the target: 55 mg/dL for LDL, since 54 mg/dL is 1.396 mmol/L.
  - The read-only summaries (the tiles, the plan cards, the form's cholesterol intro, the Lp(a) checklist and the MCP `get_plan` currentValues) print a value on the side of X the plan grades it: LDL 1.40 mmol/L reads 55 mg/dL.
  - The history matrices, blood tests and vitals, are editable, so they show plain values: re-entering the shown number writes nothing, and any other number saves (US-03 AC3). Their colour is the plan's grade. So a value within one display step of its line, entered in the other unit, can read on the other side of the hint there: LDL 1.40 mmol/L shows as 54 mg/dL beside "<55", coloured amber.
  
  Before, a tile said "Optimal" at LDL 3.2 while the plan called it above target.
- Evidence: "Why this suggestion?" expansions observed in recordings; `results_viewed` event now counts reach. AC4 added 2026-09-01 after a live reproduction: the v2 store returned ALL active rows from `loadLatestMeasurements()` (v1's server did the latest-per-metric selection), so every consumer taking the first match per metric read an arbitrary historical row — insertion order on one device, uuid order after a merge. Broken since 79afc7e (2026-06-08), all 13 LONGITUDINAL_FIELDS. Fix: `latestActivePerMetric()` in health-core, applied in the store. Reach (product-health): 2026-W32–W34 ~50/day flat; W35 261; W36 214; W37 226; W38 172 (~25/day) with the host site flat (Clarity 4,258 sessions/3d) — and a two-day trough on 2026-09-17/18 UTC (13, 12 plan views vs 21–34 on every other day) that began ~19 h before deploy run 76 and ended ~29 h after it, so the deploy does not explain it; recovered 09-19; unexplained (W38 backlog #2). W39 384 (+123%, ~55/day; 259 distinct visitors vs 134): the 09-20 spike (91 views, 73 visitors, 22 of 24 UTC hours) is people, not a scan; source unmeasured — events carry no referrer, Clarity's 3-day window had rolled past it, and the YouTube-bot's own rise came from a 09-23 video, not this day (W39 backlog #3).
- Tests: ✅ suggestions.test.ts — the LDL-without-total case is covered by `'shows LDL when neither ApoB nor non-HDL available'` (verified 2026-08-07). ✅ AC5 real WebKit on the live page, 2026-09-28 (health-roadmap-edu-92): LDL 3.2 mmol/L read "Above optimal"; total cholesterol 3.76 with HDL 2.20 read non-HDL "1.56 mmol/L Optimal" with no statin card. ✅ AC4: measurement-history.test.ts (`latestActivePerMetric`) + roadmap-store.test.ts (single-device and post-merge LDL recency, 2026-09-01). ✅ AC5 matrices (Codex, 2026-09-28): HealthTool.journey.test.tsx types the shown LDL, ApoB and waist again, each entered in the other unit just below or above its line, in the New column for its day and in its editor: nothing is written, and a different number is an exact correction. BloodTestTimeline.test.tsx and StartingInfoVitals.waist.test.tsx pin the plain value and the plan's colour.

### US-08 · Print / save my plan
As a user, I can print or save my plan as a PDF to bring to my doctor (client-side; no server involved). The guest capture button drives the same pipeline — see US-18.
- Tests: ❌ untested (print pipeline; accepted — manual verify per deploy).

## Epic C — Owning my data (local-first)

### US-09 · Choosing where my data lives
As a privacy-conscious user, I choose Google Drive / Dropbox / GitHub / my own WebDAV server / just-this-browser, from a neutral picker with no dark patterns, and can switch later without losing anything (data copies down, tokens dropped, new provider lifted).

**Positioning (constitution, 2026-08-14):** cloud sync is the **durability upgrade for the motivated**, not the front door — the funnel says typed captures outnumber cloud connects ~10:1 (US-23). The picker and its place in the flow stay exactly as they are (Brad's call, 2026-08-14); what changes is expectations: US-23, not this story, is how the mass avatar gets longitudinal value.
- AC1: OAuth (Drive/Dropbox) via PKCE redirect; GitHub/WebDAV via pasted credentials validated before commit.
- AC2: Switching providers copies current data to device first; log-off leaves the local copy intact.
- AC2b (2026-09-10): Connecting GitHub refuses a PUBLIC repository, and so does writing to one. `connect()` reads the repo's `private` flag and throws before `setJson(CONFIG_KEY, …)`, so a refused repo saves nothing and leaves nothing to reconnect from. A saved connection resumes with a bare `new GitHubAdapter()` and never calls `connect()`, so `write()` and `writeDocument()` — every write funnels through those two — re-read the flag once per adapter instance before the first PUT: one GET, shared by concurrent first writes, retried on the next write if it failed. The check fails closed; reads stay ungated. Accepted residual, stated: a repo flipped to public mid-session keeps writing until the next page load, which re-checks.
- AC3: On-device guest data migrates up on first connect (merge, not overwrite).
- AC6 (2026-09-09): Cloud-storage failure diagnostics retain operation/provider context but never document titles, refs, provider bodies, arbitrary exception text or request breadcrumbs. Local console diagnostics follow the same boundary. **Widened 2026-09-10 to the server's own console:** a Klaviyo failure logged the provider's response body to the Fly log, which is unscrubbed and where a Klaviyo error echoes the address that was submitted — both the subscribe and the suppress path now log the status and a fixed message only, and the body reaches ops through Sentry, where `scrubEventText` runs over it. **Widened again 2026-09-10 (night):** the Discord bot attached the author's Discord id to a dedup diagnostic and the author's tag to a persist failure, so an account name reached Sentry beside the message length. Both `extra` fields are gone; the conversation id, the age and the length stay, and they are enough to read the event. Tests: `app/lib/klaviyo.server.test.ts` for the Klaviyo logging; no test for the Discord change (the Discord module has no test file — the two fields were removed and verified by reading).
- AC7 (2026-09-10, from a ChatGPT audit of the server `beforeSend`): a health value written into free text never leaves the app in an error report. The audit fed the hook "My LDL is 3.2 mmol/L and I have diabetes": the LDL FIELD was redacted by the key scrub, but the sentence itself survived in the exception value, in an `extra.message` string and in console breadcrumb arguments. A text scrub now runs over every free-text field of an event on BOTH sides — server `instrument.server.mjs` and browser `sentry.ts`, one shared implementation (`scrubEventText`): `exception.values[].value`, `message`, `logentry`, every string under `extra`, `contexts` and `tags`, breadcrumb messages and data, and `request`. Rules, layered on the existing key scrub: a number with a unit → `[value]`; a number within 20 characters of a metric or lab name → `[value]`; an email address → `[email]`. Error class names, stack frames and filenames stay intact — they are the diagnostic. Server-side the console breadcrumb integration is off (console arguments are raw application text) and any `extra` string over 200 characters is dropped, key and all. Honest limits, stated not hidden: condition and drug words in prose are NOT scrubbed ("I have diabetes" survives) — no word list reads a sentence reliably, and a value-free condition word was judged the lesser risk against unreadable diagnostics; and a bare number with neither unit nor nearby metric name is left alone; fullwidth digits and date-shaped numbers pass through; and the 20-character window is blunt at both ends — it misses a metric name further away and can clip a neighbouring one (the `1` in "HbA1c was 42 mmol/mol; weight 96.4kg" is redacted because `weight` sits inside its window), which redacts more, never less, and is cosmetic. The unit and metric vocabularies are literals in two files (the pre-bundle `.mjs` cannot import the clinical engine), kept honest by `instrument-scrub-parity.test.ts`, which compares both implementations AND fails when `UNIT_DEFS` or `METRIC_LABELS` gains an entry the lists miss. **Amended 2026-09-10 (afternoon):** the key scrub redacts a family of credential keys, not one name at a time, so a new secret-shaped field is caught the day it appears; the metric and unit vocabularies are DERIVED from the clinical catalogue rather than retyped, so a new metric cannot be scrubbed in one implementation and missed in the other; and the parity test covers the whole pipeline, browser and server, not the vocabulary lists alone. Tests: `packages/health-core/src/instrument-scrub-parity.test.ts`, `packages/health-core/src/sentry-scrub.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/sentry-pipeline-parity.test.ts` **Amended 2026-09-10 (late):** a breadcrumb's `data` is now an allow-list — `method`, the URL's origin, `status_code` — instead of a scrub run over whatever the SDK attached, so a field nobody anticipated cannot ride out inside a fetch breadcrumb: a Dropbox request whose `data` carried an `input` object or a `Dropbox-API-Arg` header keeps only the three. Tests: `packages/health-core/src/instrument-scrub-parity.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/sentry-pipeline-parity.test.ts` **Amended 2026-09-10 (sixth pass):** the key list gains the families a lab report and a patient profile are actually spelled in — date of birth (`dob`, `dateOfBirth`, `date_of_birth`, `birthDate`), `gender` beside `sex`, `patientName`, and the patient identifiers `nhi` and `mrn` — plus the generic carriers a row or an extraction hangs its content on: `value`, `values`, `result`, `results`, `title`, `note`, `notes`, `summary`. No live `extra`, `tags` or `contexts` site uses any of those names for a diagnostic we read (checked across `app/` and `widget-src/`), so nothing legible is lost. And `event.request.url` is now cut to the site and the path, with the query string dropped WHOLE rather than filtered param by param, and the path itself run through the text scrub; `query_string` is deleted outright. A param allowlist could never survive the next provider: a Dropbox download names the document in `?path=`, which no list of OAuth names catches. Breadcrumbs already kept only the origin. Tests: `packages/health-core/src/instrument-scrub-parity.test.ts` (the probe payload, the Dropbox download URL), `packages/health-core/src/sentry-scrub.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/sentry-pipeline-parity.test.ts` **Amended 2026-09-10 (seventh pass):** the `navigation` breadcrumb is scrubbed too. The SDK records one on every `history.replaceState`, and the OAuth return calls `replaceState` to clear the address (`widget-src/src/storage/dropbox.ts`, `drive.ts`), so the breadcrumb written just before it carries `data.from`/`data.to` = `/roadmap/?code=…&state=…` — a live authorization code. Both fields now run through `scrubUrl`: the query goes whole, an absolute address keeps its origin and scrubbed path, a relative one keeps the path alone. Browser only; the server SDK records no history breadcrumb, so `instrument-scrub.mjs` is unchanged. Accepted residual, stated rather than hidden: an attack payload carried in a query string is no longer visible in Sentry — the 2026-08-23 sentry-fix diagnosis of JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-62 read the `exitIframe` query to reach it — while the path and the exception text still are. Test: `widget-src/src/lib/sentry-scrub.test.ts` **Amended 2026-10-01 (adversarial review of the reviewer sign-in plan):** a server transaction carries no request body, Authorization header, cookie, client address or query string either. The SDK read every incoming body and attached it, with the headers, to the 20% of transactions it samples, and `beforeSend` never sees a transaction, so a consent-form password or an `/mcp` body could leave. The http integration now reads no body (`maxIncomingRequestBodySize: "none"`), and `beforeSendTransaction` runs the same `scrubServerEvent`. The SDK also copies each cookie into span data by name, so `cookie` joins the credential key family on both sides. Server side, headers are now an allowlist, like breadcrumb data: `request.headers` and every `http.request.header.*` or `http.response.header.*` span attribute keep host, user-agent, content-type, content-length and accept, and nothing else, so an Authorization header, a cookie, a Referer carrying a page's query, a forwarding IP or an unanticipated header never leaves. The client's address goes from span data too: the SDK copies the first X-Forwarded-For hop, the visitor's own address, into `http.client_ip`, so that key and the other-end names (`client.address`, `net.peer.*`, `network.peer.*`, `user.ip_address`) are dropped. A transaction repeats the request as span attributes, so the rules follow it there. On the root span a key ending in `query` (`http.query`, `url.query`) is dropped, and a URL-valued attribute (`http.target`, `http.url`, `url.full`) goes through `scrubUrl`, as `request.url` does: the query goes whole and the path is text-scrubbed. Every child span gets the breadcrumb rule (most are express middleware and database spans, where only the peer address changes; the ones that matter are outgoing calls): a URL keeps its origin, a bare path is dropped, an `http.client` description becomes the method and origin (the SDK writes the full URL there, and a path can name a document), and its data gets the key and text scrubs the root span gets. An outgoing call that outlives its request (a fire-and-forget insert after the response) arrives as a root transaction of its own, op `http.client`; it gets the same outgoing rule, and its transaction name becomes the method and origin. An incoming request's transaction name is left as the SDK sets it: in production, under react-router-serve, it is the method and a route pattern (`GET *`), and the SDK has already cut its query; a bare Node server, or `react-router dev` with a DSN, would name it, and set `http.route`, by the raw method and path, unscrubbed (a stated residual; production is `GET *` and `POST *` only, checked over 90 days). A response's Set-Cookie is dropped by the same header allowlist. The browser still sends `document.referrer` as a Referer header (a stated residual, browser side). The header allowlist and both span passes are server-only: the browser sends no transactions and keeps its header denylist. Test: `app/lib/instrument-server-transport.test.ts` (the real SDK, the production options, real requests including a Referer, an unknown header, an X-Forwarded-For, an outgoing call to a document path and one that outlives its request; each case waits for the error event and the transactions its requests produce and checks all of them; and an event processor ahead of the scrub proves the http integration reads no body at all).
- AC8 (2026-09-10, Brad's call after the same audit): a health value on screen never reaches a session recording. Microsoft Clarity masks form FIELDS by default and keeps rendered text, so blood values on the results page were recorded. `data-clarity-mask="true"` now sits on the widget root and on every panel that portals out of it — floating chat, upload review, history dialog — because the root alone misses exactly the three surfaces carrying the most values. Clicks and scrolls still record, so the funnel signal survives. Tests: `widget-src/src/components/clarity-masking.test.tsx`. Live verification pending: theme Liquid only reproduces on the live site, and new recordings lag about an hour. **Amended 2026-09-10 (afternoon):** `ChatEmbed` carries the mask itself. It is the chat surface that renders on the blog and in the embed, outside the widget root the mask sits on, so a reply quoting the user's values was still recorded. Tests: `widget-src/src/components/clarity-masking.test.tsx`
- AC12 (2026-09-10, late — the admin and ops routes): a route that changes something does it from a POST action, never from a GET loader. The test and ops routes ran their side effects on load, so a link, a prefetch or a crawler could fire one; the side effects moved to `action` handlers and the routes are gated on `ADMIN_SHOP_DOMAIN`: when it is set, a shop that is not the named one cannot reach them at all; unset (as it is on both Fly apps today) the gate is open to any installing shop's admin. Brad sets it per app, since each app has its own shop. Tests: `app/routes/app.admin-trigger-routes.test.ts`, `app/lib/admin-shop.server.test.ts`
- AC13 (added by sentry-fix 2026-09-17, Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-6G/6K): a cloud record that cannot be read at load never leaves the mount empty. When the first read fails with a storage failure (`isStorageFailure`: a `StorageError` such as the provider refusing the token — a Google Drive grant without the `drive.file` scope answers every lookup 403 `insufficientPermissions` — or a Drive/Dropbox network failure, which those adapters wrap; a `SchemaTooNewError`; a `RecordShapeError`), the session runs on the device copy instead of rejecting before `createRoot`: Drive gets the existing "signed out / Reconnect" control, the other providers the guest view with the remembered choice kept (**superseded by AC14, 2026-09-18: every provider is now NAMED with a Retry, and no fallback session shows the guest view**), and the pending-mirror marker is set so the session's edits merge up on the next good cloud session or reconnect (AC4's machinery). A defect at startup still throws, and so does a bare `fetch` rejection from the GitHub and WebDAV adapters (a `TypeError`, not a storage failure) — stated, not covered. The failure reaches Sentry once, scrub-shaped (`area=cloud-sync op=load backend=<provider>`, fixed message; a network failure keeps the existing "Network request did not answer" wording), never with the provider's body. Before this, `main()` awaited the first read with no catch, so those users saw an empty mount on every visit until they cleared site storage. Stated residuals: the Reconnect copy says "signed out" for a grant that is alive but refusing, and for a file a newer bundle wrote; for a scope-less grant, Reconnect's popup can return the same scope-less token and drops the stored refresh token (pre-existing popup behaviour), so the user loops back here until they reconnect through the picker and grant Drive. Nothing checks the granted scope at connect time: the connect-time lift already answers 403 inside `liftLocalInto`, which swallows it by design and lets the connect count as a success — refusing there is the next fix (proposal in `docs/loops/sentry-fix/2026-09-17.md`). Tests: `widget-src/standalone/load-fallback.test.ts` (5), `widget-src/src/lib/storage-telemetry.test.ts` ("keeps the load operation").
- AC14 (added 2026-09-18, adversarial review of PR #106): nothing typed during a fallback session is lost when the cloud comes back. The device runs on the last local copy — usually an EMPTY file at `eraseEpoch` 0 — so a cloud record that has ever been erased outranks it, and `mergeFiles`' erase gate hands the cloud the file WHOLESALE: every measurement, lab value, supplement and document of that session went with no error and no event. The marker now records WHEN the session went on-device (`markSyncPending` stamps the first failure and never moves it forward while it stands), and both merges back up — `RoadmapStore.create()`'s mirror lift and the connect-time `liftLocalInto` — pass it as `mergeFiles(local, remote, { keepNewerThan })`. In the epoch-mismatch branch only, the device copy is PRUNED to the rows it stamped at or after that instant (one `ROW_STAMPS` table names the arrays and their stamps) on top of the winner's own file, and the two go back through the ordinary equal-epoch merge — so the rows meet the SAME slot, key and union rules as any other row, no slot ends with two active rows, and the epoch, the singletons and the clocks all stay the winner's. `profile`, `screenings` and `reminderOptIn` are last-write-wins singletons a stale device clock could resurrect, and `recommendationSnapshots` carries no stamp to judge by, so none of them travel: a fallback edit to the profile or the screening answers is still lost to the gate — stated, not covered. Second half of the same review: every cloud provider now gets the reconnect state, not Drive alone. Dropbox, GitHub and WebDAV used to fall back into the GUEST "choose where to save" view, which invites a user who has already placed their record to pick a second provider and orphan the first; the plan panel now says "<Provider> could not be reached", offers Retry and "Use this browser only", and the picker treats the unreachable provider as the current one — `prepareSwitch` refuses to leave a cloud it could not copy down instead of switching silently, and the copy-down it runs carries the marker too, so an erased cloud file coming DOWN cannot wipe the rows the fallback session is still holding. The marker stamps the last moment device and cloud were in step — the store's last successful load or save, and for a failed first lift the oldest row on the device — not the moment the failure surfaced, or a row typed during the debounce would be read as stale. Two residuals, stated plainly: (a) rows written between that moment and a LATER erase on another device come back by design — the merge cannot tell "typed while offline" from "typed before someone erased the record", and it prefers keeping the user's typing to honouring an erase it learns about afterwards; (b) a row synced down from a device whose clock runs fast can be stamped at or after `since` and travel past a later erase for the same reason, because no row records which device wrote it. A per-file `erasedAt` (so every writer, not just the widget, keeps genuinely post-erase rows) is the deeper fix and is not built.
