Since 2026-09-04 the same extraction pipeline also serves the connector's `import_documents` (US-35), which lands the file as a metadata-only `documents[]` row the website can archive behind. 2026-W37: 4 → 3 (one unpaired start, 0 extraction failures — third such week); 2026-W38: 1 → 1. 2026-W39: 11 → 7 with 1 extraction failure (second ever) — three silent review drops, the fourth such week. Phase 3's first `lab_unit_refused` week (first fire 2026-09-24T06:02Z; live via the manual deploy 3296cf2 of 09-23 — Brad confirmed 2026-09-27 the factor table was verified before it, closing the gate the heading had still shown): 8 rows, one visitor, one report — rbc `10^6/cu.mm` (a spelling the catalogue could take as identity), five differentials `%` (a different quantity from the count), prolactin `ng/ml` and bare urea `mg/dL` (both refused by design, AC7 and lab-catalog.ts:96); each value was shown and lost (W39 backlog #2).
- Tests: ✅ pure pipeline covered (2026-08-07): `lab-extraction.test.ts` (content blocks, classification branching, unit-resolution fallback), `zip-extract.test.ts` (junk filtering, caps, nested folders — plus a real-fixture test against Brad's `health.zip`, local-only/skipped in CI, structure-only assertions), `anthropic.server.retry.test.ts` (retry/backoff/Sentry-silencing policy), `isImage`/`isPdf` guards. ✅ AC4–6 session interactions: `UploadModal.session.test.tsx` (deferred quota, draft flushing, extraction and save; hidden errors; draft retention; cancel/retry; thrown save stays in review and retries without refiling a saved letter; connect-first skip survives Cancel; fresh sessions). ❌ Remaining: `resizeImage`/`extractFromPdf` rendering (need real DOM/canvas) and batch-API path. Session UI changes await deployment and live verification.

### US-13 · Review before save
As a user, I review extracted values in a metric×date matrix (mirroring the live timeline), can edit any value (tagged `lab_import_edited`), and only what I confirm is saved.
- AC1: Dedup: documents on `sourceFileName`, lab values on `(metric, recorded_at)` — re-uploading the same file is a no-op, not an error or duplicate. **Widened 2026-09-04 (US-35 AC6):** a document is also already-imported when a live row carries the bytes' `contentHash` (`sha256-<hex>`) — ONE dedup key shared with the connector, so a rename defeats the name check but not the hash; a hash on a row WITHOUT a `fileRef` is the connector's metadata-only import, and uploading that same PDF on the website archives the blob into a new row and tombstones the metadata-only one. **Fixed 2026-09-07 (website read-back DEFECT B):** the review step offers Save even with every value already recorded when the bytes match such a row ("Nothing new to save. Save will archive the original PDF…", button "Save 1 Original") — `ReviewTable.archive.test.tsx`, `archive-payloads.test.ts` (`connectorOriginals`). Same day, widened to every document type: a clinic letter the connector filed is name-deduped to an UNSELECTED row, so it never reached the archive step; now a hash match on a `fileRef`-less row counts it as an original too, Save is offered, and `handleSave` files it with its reviewed content (`connectorDocumentEntries`) so the store tombstones the hash-only row exactly as for a lab PDF (`roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts`, both types). Re-selecting the row saves it as a document instead, never both. **Fixed 2026-09-07 (DEFECT C, Sentry 7715862604):** a title with an em dash (or a macron, or an emoji) broke the Dropbox `Dropbox-API-Arg` header — now `dropboxApiArg()` escapes non-ASCII as `\uXXXX` for every content call (`dropbox.test.ts`, `dropbox-rest.test.ts`) — and a failed blob write behind a connector row no longer files a plain duplicate: the connector row stays live, `bulkSaveDocuments` returns `errorCount`, and the modal's "could not be saved" notice asks for a retry (`roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts`).
- AC2: Edited-at-review values carry the edited source for audit.
- AC3 (added 2026-09-03 as US-32 AC24, restated here because it is this table's behavior): when the document carries a number for a `(metric, day)` an active row already holds, the review table shows the CONFLICT — record value, document value, and a Replace checkbox UNCHECKED by default — instead of dropping the document's number; equal values are context, not a conflict; Replace is written as a correction (`correctsId`, old row `entered-in-error`, original date). Nothing auto-supersedes on source rank.
- Tests: ✅ store-level bulk-save dedup (measurements + lab values, re-upload no-op) in `roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts` (2026-08-07); ✅ AC3 `ReviewTable.conflict.test.ts` + `roadmap-store-upload-conflict.test.ts` (2026-09-03); ✅ the archive-behind-a-connector-row path in `roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts` (2026-09-04); the rest of the ReviewTable UI still 🟡.

### US-14 · Document archive
As a user, my uploaded reports/letters become organized markdown documents (+ original file when my cloud can hold it) that I can reopen in a lightbox.
- Note (2026-09-04, US-35 AC8): a file imported through the connector lands here as a metadata-only row — `fileRef: ""`, `contentHash`, sniffed `mimeType`, `extractedText: ""`, `metadata.importedVia: "connector"` — with the original left where the user put it; a lab report's row takes the shape the website's upload writes (`pathology_report`, hidden from the Documents list). The website archives the blob behind such a row on the next upload of the same bytes (US-13 AC1).
- Tests: ✅ document-path taxonomy; 🟡 archive-payloads synthesis; ❌ store deleteDocument/readDocumentFile. Deferred.

## Epic E — Chat

### US-15 · Ask about my plan
As a user, I can ask the assistant about my suggestions; it answers with my plan as context, cites Brad's content, and its name matches the store I'm on (Brad AI / MicroVitamin).

**Decided (Brad, 2026-09-10).** The blog chat bubble (`widget-src/src/site-chat.tsx`) and the chatbot embed (`widget-src/src/chatbot-embed.tsx`) both read the widget's cached inputs out of `localStorage` (`loadGuestInputs()`) and send them as context with every message, and neither bundle sets `VITE_LOCAL_FIRST`, so the server stores the whole transcript. Both open questions are answered. The cached values keep being sent: the answer is worse without them, and they are the person's own values, already stored in that browser (since AC10, 2026-09-26, that includes their saved measurements, which may have arrived by a lab import, another device or an assistant, not only what they typed). The transcripts join the 30-day purge, so the text does not outlive the audit that needs it (AC8). The README and the privacy addendum now say that.
- AC1: Server-proxied on Shopify surfaces; BYOK on Pages (chat reaches no Brad server there; the build itself still calls `api.google-token` for Google sign-in, `api.reminders-v2` if the user enrols, and Sentry for scrubbed errors).
- AC2: `chat_opened` now measures adoption.
- AC4 (2026-09-07): Main-LLM, router and classifier failure telemetry contains only bounded diagnostic categories and numeric timing/counts. User messages, assistant content, provider response bodies, author identifiers and arbitrary error strings never enter Sentry events or failure-path console logs. Conversation storage is unchanged.
- AC3: A transient upstream failure — a 5xx whose body is not our handler's JSON (the Shopify proxy / Fly edge answered, not our route) — is retried once before chat surfaces an error. The send POST is retried only within an existing conversation; a thread's FIRST message is never auto-retried (on the stored surfaces a retry could mint a ghost conversation). On the stored surfaces the server-side dedup absorbs an identical resend; on the widget (AC7, nothing stored) the client re-serves its own last reply when the resend is byte-identical to its last user turn, and otherwise a retry costs one more LLM call and no row. Added 2026-08-12 from Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-1M / -22 (sentry-fix loop; scoped per independent review); widget wording 2026-09-10.
- AC6 (2026-09-09): GET/POST/DELETE reject invalid conversation IDs before database use. Omitted/null POST IDs remain new conversations. Route error diagnostics, including detached failures, never include raw database errors or IDs. `api.chat-privacy.test.ts` checks SDK envelopes and console output.
- AC7 (2026-09-10, Brad's decision): A turn from the main widget — the surface that sends the health record with every message, identified by `localFirst: true` in the POST body, which only the `VITE_LOCAL_FIRST` builds send — writes no `chat_conversations` row and no `chat_messages` row: not the question, not the reply, not a title (titles are the first words of the question). The widget sends that conversation's earlier turns in the request from its own `chat-history.json`; the server uses them for the prompt and discards them. What the server keeps is one `chat_match_events` row per answered turn: the question text verbatim (Brad, 2026-09-10 — a number scrub was built and then dropped as over-complication: an adversarial review found nine shapes still leaking through it, so a scrub good enough to trust needed a second round of rules, and routing is about topics, not magnitudes; the audit needs the real question), the prior-turn context the router saw (on the stored surfaces only — a widget row keeps just `platform: 'widget'`, so it can never hold a second question), the matched handles, classification, router timings, and the fallback flag and category, so article matching can be audited. Honest limits: a question can name a value ("my LDL is 4.2"), so this is "no reply and no record", not "no health information". **Amended 2026-09-10 (public-repo privacy audit):** the question text is now kept for 30 days, long enough to audit article matching, and then removed; the handles it matched stay on as counts, so the audit signal outlives the text. A daily cron does the removal on every platform (`app/lib/chat-purge-cron.server.ts`, lock `chat_text_purge`): past 30 days `message` goes null, `router_context.first`/`.recent` are dropped, and `router_raw` and `router_error` are blanked too (2026-09-10 — the router's raw output is the model's answer about the question and the error text can quote it), while `matched_handles`, `classification`, the timings and `is_fallback` stay forever. `guest_chat_sessions.ip_address` holds a salted HMAC of the address (`hashClientIp`, HKDF from the app-proxy secret — equal addresses still match, no address reads back) and the session's audit entry carries no address at all, so a retained question is no longer linkable to an IP. The blog chat bubble (`site-chat`), `chatbot-embed`, Discord and YouTube store messages exactly as before, for router optimisation. `router_context.platform` names the surface on every row (`widget` / `shopify` / `discord` / `youtube`); the `chat_match_events → chat_messages` and `→ chat_conversations` foreign keys are gone, so a user's delete no longer removes router telemetry. A widget row carries only a whitelisted set of columns (`redactForWidget`), so a reply or error text cannot ride along unseen. Usage signal: daily `chat_match_events` rows with `router_context->>'platform' = 'widget'` against the prior 7-day shopify total (widget and blog were one number before this change), `chat_opened` steady, Sentry "Chat sendMessage failed" flat. No new `product_events` name: `trackProductEvent` fires once per session per name, so a per-turn event cannot live there. **The history went with the decision (Brad, 2026-09-10):** the 266 widget conversations stored before it — 211 guest-owned plus 55 from before the local-first cutover — were deleted on 2026-09-10 (the statement lives in `supabase/rls-policies.sql`). The 8 profile-owned blog conversations were kept: that surface still stores transcripts, and Brad wants them for router optimisation.
- AC8 (2026-09-10, Brad's decision): The stored Shopify transcripts join the 30-day window AC7 built. The same daily cron (`app/lib/chat-purge-cron.server.ts`, lock `chat_text_purge`) runs two more passes under the predicate `platform in ('shopify','discord')`, reached from `chat_messages` through a PostgREST `!inner` embed so a message qualifies only on its own conversation's platform. Past 30 days by `created_at`, `chat_messages.content` goes null; past 30 days by `updated_at`, `chat_conversations.title` goes null, because a title is the first words of the question. A thread resumed after that shows as Untitled: the title is set on insert only and is not refilled. YouTube rows are never selected: those turns are public comments on a video, under their own retention. **Amended 2026-09-10 (evening), Brad's decision:** Discord joined the predicate. A question typed at the bot is as private as one typed into the bubble, so its transcripts are blanked on the same clock; only the platform list changed, not the passes. Discord user ids stay on the rows — the decision covers the text. The bot's own history load (`loadConversationHistory` in `app/lib/discord-bot.server.ts`) drops null-content rows before building context, the way the POST reload does, so a purged turn is never sent to the model and never dedups against a blank. What stays is the shape of the thread, not the words: the row id, `role`, `created_at`, `model`, the token counts, `is_fallback` and `failure_mode`, so the fallback and cost audits outlive the text exactly as the handle counts do. A blanked turn reads back as `PURGED_TEXT` = `[removed after 30 days]` in the GET messages response and in the audit renderer (`tools/chat-audit-pull.ts`); the POST history reload drops null-content rows instead, so a purged turn is never sent to the model and never dedups against a blank. `chat_messages.content` drops its NOT NULL in `supabase/rls-policies.sql`. The cached widget inputs keep being sent as chat context (the header decision above). Tests: `app/lib/chat-purge-cron.server.test.ts` (Shopify and Discord rows blanked, other columns kept, YouTube untouched, in-window untouched on both platforms, title only on stale conversations, idempotent, update error throws, no-progress guard throws) and `app/routes/api.chat-privacy.test.ts` (GET substitutes the placeholder; POST answers from the live turns only).
- AC5 (2026-09-07): Sentry accepts errors from the current Shopify main widget, lazy chunks, upload and chat bundles, and our Pages scripts. Unrelated scripts and misleading/rehosted URLs stay excluded. `sentry-bundles.test.ts` runs the real SDK filter with the production init options against the emitted build files.
- AC9 (2026-09-10, from Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-6E, sentry-fix loop): A `/api/chat` request without a valid app-proxy signature, once it reaches the proxy check, is answered with the 400 the Shopify library throws, never a 500, and is never a Sentry error (a bare GET with neither `logged_in_customer_id` nor `sessionToken` still takes the pre-existing empty-list return before any check runs — it reads nothing). `authenticate.public.appProxy` rejects by throwing a `Response`; the route's catch-alls re-throw a thrown `Response` so react-router returns it as-is, and only failures in our own code reach `reportChatError`. Before this AC two unsigned `curl` POSTs straight at the Fly host (2026-09-10 08:51Z) became "Chat: Action failed" events and 500s — the same issue id a real action failure would land under, and that message carries no cause by design (AC4/AC6). `api.chat.test.ts` pins both the POST and the GET path.
- AC10 (2026-09-26, Brad approved; wording corrected 2026-09-28 after adversarial review): The blog chat bubble and the chatbot embed send the saved record: the saved profile, the latest saved value of each measurement with its date, and the medications and screenings on record. Nothing typed but unsaved is sent. (The widget's own chat sends the same set, plus up to 24 dated values per measurement and what is typed on the page but not yet saved.) The unit system is the one the widget shows: a saved preference, else the detected one. After an erase the copy is emptied and the embed mutes (US-11 AC8). An erase made on another device reaches this copy only once the widget loads here. The copy of medications and screenings is refreshed from the store after each save, so a failed save leaves nothing behind. So a returning user, whose form fields are empty because the values are saved, gets answers with their numbers. The embed is not muted for a returning user with a saved weight, or with only blood tests. It reads the context when each message is sent, not when the page loads. The cached copy has one meaning, the saved record: HealthTool's medication and screening handlers no longer write unsaved form values into it. The context goes into the prompt only; nothing stores it (checked 2026-09-26: `api.chat.ts` and `chat.server.ts` write only the question, the reply and router telemetry). A reply can still quote the values, as AC8 and the privacy addendum already say.
- AC11 (2026-09-28, from the /simplify altitude review): The chat's plan counts the medications and screenings on record.
  - **The bug:** before this AC, the Shopify chat server read the medication and screening lists as flat objects. Both clients send lists, so the server got nothing, and it computed the plan as if none were recorded. Given rosuvastatin 40 mg and ApoB 1.0, the model was handed a "start a statin" suggestion. The Pages chat already converted the lists, so the two chats disagreed.
  - **Now:** one health-core builder feeds both. Every field is sanitized before it can reach the prompt:
    - medication keys are allowlisted;
    - numbers are bounded;
    - a screening date must be a real calendar date (month 01 to 12, and a day that exists in that month and year): `screeningsToInputs` drops any other, for the widget's plan, the chat, the reminder schedule and `get_plan`'s `due` list alike, so '2020-99' can no longer roll forward and read as "up to date", or schedule a reminder. A "last done" date (the six `_last_date` keys) must also fall in the current month or before it, as the form's own year and month lists do; the five `_followup_date` keys keep accepting future dates, because the form asks when a follow-up is scheduled;
    - strings are capped.
  - **Drug names:** a listed name passes through. An unlisted name still counts in the plan, exactly as the widget reads it, but the prompt shows it as "unlisted". The prompt never says "other", which is the form's own "Other GLP-1" choice and means something to the plan. An empty name is left out of the prompt, and a non-string name (null, a number or an object in a hand-edited file) drops its row, as `medicationsToInputs` does for the widget's plan. A name outside the character bound becomes a placeholder that means nothing to the plan. A test pins that no suggestion title carries a drug name, so an unlisted name never reaches the model.
  - **Doses:** the plan reads the raw dose, as the widget does. The prompt drops a dose outside its range.
  - **Data sent:** the browser sends only the fields the builder uses: no row ids and no timestamps.
  - **The values the model reads are the values the plan used** (Codex, 2026-09-28): `latestValues` come from the snapshot the plan was computed from, so an unsaved value the widget sends stands in for the saved one, and the dated series fills only the fields the snapshot lacks. Before, the saved series overwrote the snapshot, so the model could explain a plan built on 3.4 while being told 1.0.
  - **Both chats read the same context (round 3, 2026-09-28):** the Pages chat plans from the context the widget supplies, with unsaved typed values and lent drafts, as the Shopify chat does (AC10). Every chat on the page supplies that context, so the old fallback to the saved file was dead code and is gone.
- AC12 (2026-09-29, chat audit F4): on the brand store the chat title, subtitle and empty state name products, not a plan. Shop metafield `health_roadmap.chat_surface` (`doctor` default, `brand`) reaches `ChatSection` via `data-surface`. The chat dialog's accessible name follows too: "<assistant name> chat" on brand, "Health by Dr Brad Chat" on doctor. Tests: `ChatSection.test.tsx`, `assistant-config.test.ts`.
- AC13 (2026-09-29, chat audit F1): one source of truth for models. `packages/health-core/src/models.ts` pins the answer (`CHAT_MODEL`, Sonnet 5.5), router, classifier and extraction models, and `modelParams` builds each family's request shape: Haiku takes `temperature`; the Sonnet 5 family takes no sampling param, turns thinking off with `between_tools` on 5.5 and `disabled` on 5, and thinks adaptively with `output_config.effort`; any other id throws rather than guess a shape. Server code and every harness (router, answer check, classifier, tool edits, YouTube dry run) import it, so the harnesses test the model production runs: the router and classifier harnesses default to thinking off, as production (`--effort-low` is the candidate arm), and the answer check sends the chat's edit tools. `--model` / `--answer-model` qualify a candidate; `--category` takes a comma list. A fetch timeout or network failure counts as an API error in the router, answer-check and classifier harnesses, as a non-2xx does: visible, counted, and the run goes on (the tool-edits and YouTube dry-run harnesses still throw on one; stated, not covered). A router case's `must_not_route` fails it when any run returns a listed handle, so an extra handle can fail a case. BYOK chat stays on Haiku by design (the user pays). Tests: `models.test.ts`, `chat.server.refusal.test.ts` (the answer body), `tools/test-chatbot-matching.test.ts` (an answer-check timeout is counted and the run exits 1; `--category a,b` runs both categories' cases).
- AC14 (2026-09-29, chat audit F2): a refusal (HTTP 200, `stop_reason: 'refusal'`) is a third outcome beside answer and fallback. The model's partial text is discarded; the user sees a fixed line: for `bio`, `general_harms` and unknown categories "I can't help with that here. Please raise it with your doctor or pharmacist."; for `reasoning_extraction`, `cyber` and `frontier_llm` "I can't help with that request." Web returns it as the reply with `isRefusal: true` and `isFallback: true` (the flag the widget's dedup reads; the widget stores it in the cloud chat history, so it holds after a reload); Discord posts it; no surface re-serves it on a duplicate send. On the streaming web surface, the partial text already shown is replaced by the refusal line when the refusal arrives; on the surfaces that post only a finished reply (Discord, YouTube) it is never posted. Rows carry `failure_mode = 'refusal'` with `is_fallback` false. Telemetry carries only the stop reason and the closed category (`chat_timing`, the Sentry warning tags), never text. A `max_tokens` stop keeps the answer, adds nothing the user sees, and logs its stop reason; a tool call it cut off mid-input is dropped, and a stop with no text is the fallback, logged with its stop reason (never a parse error). Tests: `anthropic.server.retry.test.ts`, `anthropic.server.stream.test.ts`, `chat.server.refusal.test.ts`, `api.chat-widget.test.ts`, `chat-dedup.server.test.ts`, `chat-api.local-first.test.ts`, `chat-history-store.test.ts`.
- AC15 (2026-09-29, chat audit F2): on YouTube a refusal skips the comment and keeps its claim, like SKIP_NO_REPLY; only a main-LLM failure is unclaimed for the next tick. Before, a refusal ran as a failure; by the code (inferred, not observed in the logs), the same comment would have been retried every 30 minutes for up to 7 days, three model calls each time. A refusal sends the web chat's bounded Sentry warning (`platform: youtube`). Tests: `youtube-bot.server.test.ts` (`completionOutcome`, `shouldUnclaim`).
- AC16 (2026-09-29, Brad's decision, chat audit F3): the web chat streams the answer. The widget POSTs `stream: true`; the route answers `application/x-ndjson`, one line per thinking or text delta, then a `done` line carrying exactly the JSON the unstreamed reply would have been. A refusal or fallback `done` line carries `isFallback: true`, and the client replaces what streamed with its content. The body decides the path, not the header: an `application/json` answer (an older server, an error status) takes the unstreamed path unchanged; any other 200 is read as NDJSON when its first line starts `{"type":`, so a proxy that rewrote the type still streams, and fails as before otherwise. Without `stream: true` the route is unchanged. Persistence and telemetry run after the stream completes; `chat_timing` adds `streamed` and `firstTokenMs`. Discord, YouTube and the Pages BYOK chat do not stream. `CHAT_STREAMING=false` on the server is the off switch: the route ignores `stream: true` and answers JSON, which the client reads unchanged (default on; docs/deploy-runbook.md). A stream cut off before `done` is reported as a network error and not retried, because the server finishes and persists the turn after a disconnect, so a retry would duplicate it on the stored surfaces; the pre-stream transient retry (AC3) is unchanged. Tests: `app/lib/anthropic.server.stream.test.ts`, `app/lib/chat.server.refusal.test.ts`, `app/routes/api.chat-stream.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/chat-api.stream.test.ts`.
- AC17 (2026-09-29, Brad's decision, chat audit F3): the sending tab shows the model's thinking summary live in a panel labelled "Thinking (draft notes, not the answer)" once the first thinking delta arrives, replacing the ten canned lines; before that, and on a path that never streams (BYOK, a buffering proxy), the panel says "Thinking…" only; the polite live region (`aria-live="polite"`) is a short static status line ("Thinking…", then "Answering…"), never the growing panel; other tabs keep the pulsing dots. When the answer lands, the summary folds into a collapsed "Show how I approached this" toggle above it. It is kept only for the answer as it was streamed in this tab: reopening the thread from History shows the answer without it. The summary is never persisted: not on the server, not in the user's cloud chat history, not synced to other tabs, never logged; a fallback or refusal keeps none. Usage signal: `chat_thinking_opened`, fired once per answer when the toggle is opened (the client's per-tab-session throttle then keeps one per session). Tests: `widget-src/src/hooks/useChatState.test.tsx`, `widget-src/src/components/ChatSection.streaming.test.tsx`.
- AC18 (2026-09-29, chat audit §4.3): `tools/check-newer-model.ts` lists the Models API and exits 0 when no `claude-sonnet-*` is newer than `CHAT_MODEL`, 10 when one is, and 1 on a missing key, an API error or a pin missing from the list. On 10 it prints the answer-hop qualification commands (`test-chatbot-matching.ts --answer-model <id> --answer-check`, `test-tool-edits.ts --model <id>`), then the router and classifier lines, marked "only if the router or classifier pin is changed". The product-health loop runs it weekly (it needs `ANTHROPIC_TEST_API_KEY`, which Brad grants to that loop) and opens the bump as a `claude/` PR labelled `hold`, for Brad's merge, never auto-shipped: the bump changes clinical answers, and the harness cannot catch a fabricated citation. Tests: `tools/check-newer-model.test.ts`.
- AC19 (2026-09-29, second-round review): the form tools go only where a client applies them. The widget's own chat (the `ChatSection`/`ChatEmbed` instance given `onProposeEdit`) sends `canApplyEdits: true`; `/api/chat` sends `CHAT_EDIT_TOOLS` only then. The blog bubble, the chatbot embed, Discord and YouTube send no tools, so no reply there says "Done — I've updated your medications" for an edit nothing applies. The Pages BYOK chat runs only inside the app and keeps its tools. `tools` render before `system`, so a request with tools and one without share no prompt-cache prefix and cache separately. Tests: `app/lib/chat.server.refusal.test.ts`, `app/routes/api.chat-stream.test.ts`, `app/lib/chat-surfaces-tools.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/chat-api.stream.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/hooks/useChatState.test.tsx`.
- AC20 (2026-09-29, Brad, measured live: "what's the ideal ApoB" showed only "Thinking…" for 7.4 s, because the stream opened after the classifier, router and content loading, and Sonnet 5.5 often sends no thinking summary for a simple question): a streamed turn opens its stream before stage 1. Its first line is `{"type":"status","text":"Reading your question"}`; `Finding relevant articles` follows only when the router fires; once content loads, `{"type":"sources","titles":[…]}` names the articles actually loaded, by their titles in `docs/blog/index.json` (never model output, no second file read), subject to AC25's title rule, and is omitted when none loaded; `Writing the answer` comes just before the model call. Each status is a fixed server string. A failure after the stream opens (creating the conversation, saving the message, any throw) is a `{"type":"done","success":false,"error":…}` line; the JSON path keeps its HTTP 500. Errors before the stream opens (a bad body, an unsigned request, a guest over the cap) and the dedup re-serve stay HTTP answers. Old clients ignore the new line types. Tests: `app/routes/api.chat-stream.test.ts`, `app/lib/chat.server.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/lib/chat-api.stream.test.ts`.
- AC21 (2026-09-29): the sending tab shows the latest status (with a trailing "…") as the pending panel's label until thinking or answer text arrives; thinking switches the label to "Thinking (draft notes, not the answer)", and answer text switches the live status line to "Answering…" as before. Under the label, while pending, a small muted line reads `Reading: Title A · Title B`; when answer text starts with no thinking summary, the panel goes and the line stays, above the answer. The polite live region announces each status change as the same short static string, never the thinking or the titles. Tests: `widget-src/src/components/ChatSection.streaming.test.tsx`, `widget-src/src/hooks/useChatState.test.tsx`.
- AC22 (2026-09-29): after the answer lands, the "Show how I approached this" toggle appears when the answer had thinking OR sources, and lists `Articles read: …` above the thinking summary. The articles read follow the thinking summary's rules (AC17): this tab and this session only, never in `messages`, the cloud chat history, other tabs, logs, Sentry or product events; a fallback or refusal keeps neither. `chat_thinking_opened` still fires once per answer when the toggle opens; it now also counts answers that had sources but no thinking. Usage signal: `chat_thinking_opened`, and `chat_timing.firstTokenMs`: the wait for the first answer text, measured from the answer-model call, not from the stream's open. The preparation before that call stays in the existing per-stage fields (`classifierMs`, `routerMs`, `contextMs`, `preLlmMs`). Tests: `widget-src/src/components/ChatSection.streaming.test.tsx`, `widget-src/src/hooks/useChatState.test.tsx`.
- AC23 (2026-09-29, knowledge-refresh plan F3; the vitamin C and omega-3 references, 83,150 and 81,830 chars, never loaded under the old 80,000 cap and its `break`): every entry the router returns loads into the answer context when it fits the cap; an entry that does not fit is skipped, not the rest of the list; the cap is 120,000 chars so any single entry in the corpus fits. At 120K no two of the 29 references over 60,000 chars fit together, so when the router returns two of them the second is skipped (checked 2026-09-29 on the smallest such pair, mangosteen 60,172 + choline 60,206 = 120,378, and on vitamin C 83,150 + omega-3 81,830 = 164,980). One Sentry-free loader (`app/lib/matched-content.ts`) serves the server and both harnesses. Tests: `app/lib/chat.server.test.ts`.
- AC24 (2026-09-29, knowledge-refresh plan F3, Codex review): the router harness has a fixed-handle answer-check mode. A `tools/test-queries.json` entry with `answer_handles` and no `expected` is selected by `answer_handles` and, under `--answer-check`, its answer is scored against context built from exactly those handles through the production loader, with no router call. Before any API call the harness checks that every handle has a file and that the set fits the cap, and exits otherwise. These entries score on their own line ("Fixed-handle answer checks: n/N"), stay out of the router pass rate, variance and the loop's denominator. They gate the exit code only under `--fixed-handles-only`, where every one must pass for exit 0; a plain `--answer-check` (the model-bump qualification run) prints the line but does not fail on it, because no live baseline exists yet and a fixture can trip on its own context (the lithium reference names Grokipedia, which that fixture forbids). `--fixed-handles-only` implies `--answer-check`, runs only these entries and skips the router warmup. The harness exits unless run from the repo root, since the shared loader reads from the working directory. Tests: `tools/test-chatbot-matching.test.ts`.
- AC25 (2026-09-29; across runs, "I feel anxious all the time" got youth, palliative, perinatal and adult suicide pathways, and "Articles read:" showed them): a display rule. Population pathway titles (children, pregnancy, palliative) appear in "Reading:" and "Articles read:" only when the question, with the earlier turns the router saw, carries the matching signal; the palliative words are router rule 2's closed list. Crisis pathways (suicide, self-harm) are loaded but never titled. The loaded content is never changed by this rule: every routed pathway still reaches the answer model, so a signal the list misses hides a title and nothing more (a content filter lost the pathway for crisis phrasings, "I'm 34 weeks and have a bad headache" and "my two year old has a fever"; adversarial review, 2026-09-29). Handles are marked by words in the handle itself, and an entry naming adults or women is never marked; a pathway whose population appears only in its index title (Croup, Fentanyl Guide (Palliative Care), Recurrent Miscarriage and seven more) is marked by name. When every title is hidden, no `sources` line is sent. Usage signal: the existing `chat_match_events` miss trend. Tests: `app/lib/chat-router-guard.test.ts`, `app/routes/api.chat-stream.test.ts`.
- AC26 (2026-10-04, Brad's decision; on 2026-09-27 two pasted analysis templates were stored at exactly 500 characters, cut mid-word before any lab value): a pasted lab report or "act as a doctor" template reaches the model whole. The web chat input and the server both take a message of up to 8,000 characters (`MAX_CHARS` in `useChatState.ts`, `MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH` in `chat.server.ts`); the old cap was 500, and the widget cut longer pastes silently. 8,000 is a ceiling against abuse and runaway cost on a public guest endpoint. A longer paste keeps its first 8,000 characters and the chat says "Your message was cut at 8,000 characters."; the next edit within the cap, or a send, clears the notice. The counter reads n/8000, and the server answers a longer message with a 400. No cut splits a surrogate pair: an emoji astride the cut is dropped (`cutText`). Earlier turns are bounded per path: the widget cuts each prior turn it sends at `MAX_HISTORY_TURN_CHARS` (8,000, never below the message cap); the stored surfaces' history is bounded by the server's token budget, and BYOK history by the 20-message count, with no per-turn cut on either. The router and classifier keep their own 2,000-character slice (`sanitizeForRouter`), unchanged: they need only the topic. The router telemetry row does not grow with the message: `chat_match_events` keeps at most the first 500 characters of the question, and of the earlier turns in its router context, on every web surface (`MATCH_EVENT_MESSAGE_CHARS` in `api.chat.ts`), as it did before; the router still reads 2,000. Accepted consequence, confirmed by Brad 2026-10-04: the stored surfaces (blog bubble, chatbot embed) now keep transcripts of up to 8,000 characters per message in `chat_messages` for the 30-day AC8 window, up from 500. Reply length is the chat prompt's business, not this AC's. Usage signal: `messageChars` on the `chat_timing` log line, the length as a number and no text, shows how often a message passes 500 and 2,000. Tests: `app/routes/api.chat-widget.test.ts` (7,999 and 8,000 accepted and passed to the model whole, 8,001 refused, the 500-char row, the `messageChars` signal), `app/routes/api.chat-stream.test.ts`, `app/lib/chat.server.test.ts`, `app/lib/chat-router.server.test.ts`, `packages/health-core/src/chat-history.test.ts`, `widget-src/src/hooks/useChatState.test.tsx` (the cap, the notice, the emoji cut).
- Evidence: 926 messages; ~48% router unmatched; ~17% of queries are store-support questions (redirect opportunity); zero conversations link to a Shopify customer id (needs investigation). 2026-W32: 158 messages/wk (+394% WoW); unmatched hit 73% that week; `chat_opened` has emitted 0 events ever — the only emit site is unreachable on every live surface (dead instrumentation, fix diff in loops/product-health/2026-W32.md). 2026-W33: 260 messages (+150%, window-clean); 70.4% of match events carry no handles BUT 92/119 are deliberate `router_skipped` classifier bypasses — TRUE router misses 27/77 = 35%, below the ~48% baseline (the 73%/48% comparison conflated the two); `chat_opened` STILL 0 — the 08-10 ChatSection fix reaches only the widget bundle, because `vite.config.site-chat.ts` never defines `VITE_SHOPIFY_SURFACE` so `trackProductEvent` no-ops in the site-wide FAB bundle (loops/product-health/2026-W33.md, Tier 3 PR #23). 2026-W34: `chat_opened` LIVE — 29 events, the first ever at 2026-08-15T22:52:36Z, ~3 min after PR #23's deploy finished (production-telemetry verification); 335 messages (+29%); TRUE router misses 40/82 = 49% — back at the ~48% baseline after W33's 35%. 2026-W35: chat_opened 30 (steady); 331 messages (flat); the YouTube comment bot is now 87% of match events, so web-chat router n is tiny (6 matched / 3 true misses at n=30) — segment before trending. 2026-W36: chat_opened 25 (moved with reach); 152 messages (-54%, the YouTube bot's ebb: 194 → 61 match events) while web chat held at ~24; Discord appeared as a third `router_context` platform (8 events); blended true misses 17/42 = 40%. 2026-W37: chat_opened 28; 200 messages; 6 `router_error` rows (all YouTube, the first since W34). 2026-W38: chat_opened 24; 81 messages (53 user); match events 58 (youtube 37 / shopify 13 / widget 5 / discord 3), true misses 6/18 = 33%; router_error 0.
- Tests: ✅ server-side chat.server tests + router fixture suites (`tools/test-queries.json` Phase D loop); ✅ `widget-src/src/lib/sentry-bundles.test.ts` (AC5); ✅ `app/routes/api.chat.test.ts` (AC4 malformed body; AC9 unsigned POST and GET, failing-first 2026-09-10); 🟡 `ChatSection.test.tsx` covers both `chat_opened` emit paths and `widget-src/vite-configs.test.ts` pins the per-bundle `VITE_SHOPIFY_SURFACE` surface flags in both directions (AC2); ❌ remaining chat UI/hooks untested (accepted for now). (Status refreshed 2026-08-16, Brad-authorized live.) ✅ AC10/AC11 (2026-09-28): `widget-src/src/chat-context.test.tsx` (the bubble and embed payloads, read at send time, mute and stage rules, unit system), `HealthTool.chat-copy.test.tsx` (the mirror holds only the saved record; erase, failed save and reload race), `packages/health-core/src/chat-context.test.ts` (medications and screenings reach the plan; parity with the widget for listed, unlisted and out-of-bound drug names; injection strings never reach the prompt), `app/lib/chat.server.test.ts` and `byok-chat.test.ts` (both chats share one builder). ✅ Real WebKit on the live page, 2026-09-28 (health-roadmap-edu-92, POST aborted): the widget chat's payload carried each medication as `medicationKey`, `drugName` and `doseValue` only, with no ids, timestamps or lamport clocks, and a history dated by day.

### US-16 · Chat can fill my form
As a user, when I tell the chat my numbers ("my LDL is 3.2"), it proposes structured form edits that I confirm — it never silently writes data.
- AC1 (2026-09-25): one reply can carry several field edits: "120/80" is a systolic and a diastolic, and a weight can come with a waist. Each edit the page has a place for lands in the form, and the next save saves them all. Known gap (adversarial review, 2026-09-25): a blood-test edit sent before the blood-test matrix is on the page (a first-time user who has not given a weight yet, form stage 2) is dropped, and the chat still says it pre-filled it; an LDL alone is dropped the same way. It predates this AC; holding the edit until the matrix mounts is the fix owed. A chat value pre-filled into the vitals matrix is typed into its cell in the unit the chat stated, and the focus stays there: it no longer jumps to the email field, which on a phone pulled the page to the plan tab, away from the cell the user was asked to review. Found by the 2026-09-25 cleanup review: for a first-time user each edit rebuilt the form from the same copy, so only the last survived, and the next save wrote half a blood pressure.
- Tests: ✅ chat-edits parsing + the paid tool-edit harness (`tools/test-tool-edits.json`). ✅ 2026-09-25: `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (one reply carrying a blood pressure, or a weight and a waist, leaves both in the first-time fields, and a save saves both).

### US-43 · The chatbot's knowledge base stays consistent (weekly lint) — **added 2026-09-29 (Brad, knowledge-refresh plan decision 6); deterministic half built and run, model half built and not yet run**
As a user asking the chatbot, the pathway, the supplement reference and the plan my answer draws on agree with each other, or they differ where Brad chose to; as Brad, a weekly check finds the contradictions and the knowledge base gets fixed, while my algorithm is only reported.
- AC1 (deterministic rules; no model, and no network unless asked): `tools/knowledge-lint.ts` checks every entry in `docs/blog`, `docs/pathway` and `docs/guideline` for citation markers with no reference line and reference lines nothing cites; DOI and PMID shape, and links whose text and target differ; bodies that cite Grokipedia, with the sentences resting only on it; a number plus unit in the `index.json` summary the body never states (the refresh plan's AC2 tokeniser); and product mentions above the stored baseline. `--check-links` sends HEAD requests for the due entries' DOIs and PMIDs, 1 s apart, capped at 500.
- AC2 (the report fits the loop): counts come first and the markdown report never passes 150 lines, however many findings; `--json` holds every finding.
- AC3 (the selector): `docs/loops/chat-health/lint-state.json` holds the last run date, the slice cursor, a hash per algorithm topic and, per entry, a content hash and product baseline. The due list is every entry new or changed since the last saved run, every entry paired with an algorithm topic whose text changed, and one of 13 stable hash slices, so a full pass takes 13 weeks. `--dry-run` prints it and writes nothing. Only `knowledge-lint-compare.ts --run` advances it: every comparison attempt is recorded, a missing, refused, truncated or unparseable answer marks it retry and leaves cursor and hashes alone, three in a row park it (listed, no longer blocking), a comparison's id carries both excerpt hashes so a body rewritten mid-cycle is compared again, and a capped run works through what is owed, oldest attempt first, over the following weeks. Dead links are checked once per identifier and reported against every entry that cites one. Algorithm-side findings print with both quotes, each cut to 200 characters, never dropped.
- AC4 (divergences are deliberate and written down): every rule, deterministic or model, consults `lint-allowlist.json`; a handle entry with no rule covers every rule and model comparison on that handle, and the compare step counts the model findings it drops; an entry missing its rule, its handle or pair, its reason, date or who stops the run. The loop proposes entries and never edits the file.
- AC5 (the model half has a known cost): `tools/knowledge-lint-compare.ts` builds the entry-versus-algorithm, pathway-versus-reference and reference-versus-reference prompts for the due entries and prints the token estimate and cost at the pinned Sonnet 5.5 price (`PRICES` in `models.ts`) before anything runs. It calls the API only under `--run`, refuses `--run` without a `--max-calls` cap, refuses a `--max-usd` that is not a positive number, and before each call stops if the spend so far plus that call's worst case (estimated input plus the output cap) would pass it. A response that fails to parse, or carries any finding with a missing or malformed quote, side, handle or severity or an unverifiable quote, is rejected whole: that pair's retry, keeping none of its findings and clearing nothing; every other pair's result is kept. An instruction-shaped quote leaves a `WARN` line naming the handle and the span (cut to 120 characters), never a queue item. A model item resolves only when its pair was compared again with both quotes inside the excerpts sent; otherwise it keeps its status and records `unverified`. A dead-link item resolves only on a run that got a 2xx/3xx for that identifier on behalf of that entry in this week's slice (a timeout, network error or 5xx is inconclusive), and every item a run covers records `last_checked`. A reference excerpt takes its dosing, intake, upper-level, safety and interaction sections first, and every side records characters sent against characters available, printed as a `CUT` line when it did not fit. The first `--run` waits for Brad's written yes on the cost line.
- AC6 (external text is data): excerpts and their labels sit inside the data frame; a closing tag inside one is escaped; the model has no tools. Injected text cannot reach the model as instructions; a planted finding is caught by the batch protocol (Opus writer, AC2 raw quotes, Brad's sign-off), not by this tool. The tool keeps a finding only if both quotes are verbatim in their excerpts and its fix names one of the two handles, drops one whose quote reads like an instruction, and flags instruction-shaped text rather than following it.
- AC7 (who fixes what): every knowledge-side finding joins `lint-fix-queue.json` once: model findings from `--run`, deterministic ones under `--queue` with their rule name, deduped across weeks by rule, handle and item (a repeat refreshes `last_seen`). Algorithm-side findings go to the report only, and a pathway against the algorithm is always algorithm-side; any other fix aimed at a pathway body is report-only, never queued. A fixed or resolved item that recurs becomes `regressed`; an open or regressed one that a run covered and did not see becomes `resolved` with the date; the Grokipedia item's detail carries the counts; both tools print open, regressed and resolved counts. The loop reports and queues; a build session (Fable orchestrating, Opus writers) fixes queue items under US-42's batch protocol (AC1 to AC8), and Brad signs each batch. No lint step edits `health_roadmap_algorithm.md`, `evidence.ts`, `roadmap_text.html`, `index.json` or the allow-list.
- Usage signal (declared): `metrics.csv` `lint_*` rows and the queue's open count, falling quarter over quarter.
- Baseline (2026-09-29, corrected run after the adversarial review; allow-list of 2 entries): 1,024 entries; 87 unresolved markers in 5 entries (73 in `diet`, whose markers point to the AHA paper's list), 207 uncited reference lines in 39, 39 bibliography-only lists, 1 DOI shape, 1 PMID shape, 3 links whose text differs from the target, 67 bodies citing Grokipedia (506 reference entries), 12 summary dose mismatches in 10 entries (now counting %, mmHg and pmol/L). Estimated model cost: about $0.27 this week; about $3.83 for the 13 weekly slices ($9.37 at most); $2.80 for one `--all` pass.
- Tests: ✅ `tools/knowledge-lint.test.ts` (AC1 to AC4 and the deterministic half of AC7 on fixtures, plus the corpus shapes the first run surfaced). ✅ `tools/knowledge-lint-compare.test.ts` (AC3, AC5 to AC7; every fetch is a stub, no test calls the API). ⏳ The first `--run` waits for the test key's cap to lift (2026-10-01) and Brad's yes on the cost line.

### US-45 · The chatbot answers Potassium Fiber questions from the signed label — **added 2026-10-07 (Brad approved: "build those, then deploy")**
As someone who asks either chatbot about the Potassium Fiber drink, I get the label's real numbers and its warning, no health promise, and, on Dr Brad's education surfaces, no sales pitch and a plain statement that he profits from it. Source: `docs/products.md` § "Potassium Fiber Drink" (the Makers Nutrition panel Brad signed on 2026-10-06, and its editor notes).
- AC1 (facts): the bot quotes the panel as written: 8 g of fiber (29% Daily Value) from 10 g of acacia gum, a scoop of about 15 g, 500 mg of potassium as potassium citrate (11%), 45 calories. It never gives the pre-panel figures: 10 g of fiber, 36%, a 12.9 g scoop, 20 calories.
- AC2 (the warning decides): when the user has kidney disease, has been told to limit potassium, or takes an ACE inhibitor, an angiotensin receptor blocker, a potassium-sparing diuretic or another potassium supplement, the bot gives the label's 500 mg and sends them to their doctor. It never clears the drink for them or suggests it, including on the brand surface when they ask which product to buy.
- AC3 (no health claims): nothing ties the drink or its doses to blood pressure, the heart, cholesterol, the gut, bowel regularity, a prebiotic effect or blood sugar. Nutrient-content statements only ("high in fiber", "a good source of potassium"). Saying the drink makes no such claim is fine. Asked whether the drink helps any of these or any condition, the bot says it makes no health claims and is sold for its nutrient content, gives the per-scoop amounts, and does not summarise potassium or fiber outcome research; no answer about the drink cites an outcome study (orchestrator ruling, 2026-10-07, after a sampled brand answer summarised potassium blood-pressure trials). General potassium or fiber questions that don't mention the drink are unaffected.
- AC4 (surfaces): the doctor surfaces (drstanfield.com, YouTube, Discord) never volunteer, recommend or link the drink, never name a store and give no price unless asked the cost. They name it only when the user does, answer the factual question, and say that Brad owns the company that sells it and profits from its sale. The brand surface (microvitamin.com) may describe it and link https://microvitamin.com/products/potassium-fiber-drink, with no outcome claims; the brand posture's structure/function wording and its "MicroVitamin is a daily multivitamin" line do not apply to the drink.
- AC5 (never organic): the bot never calls the product organic. Only one ingredient's name, Inavea™ Essential Organic Talh, holds the word.
- AC6 (the harness tests a surface and a claim): a `tools/test-queries.json` entry may carry `surface: "doctor" | "brand"`. The answer check then adds that surface's posture file (`app/lib/chat-posture-<surface>.md`) after the products block and before any articles, the order production's `buildSystemBlocks` uses. An unknown surface stops the harness before any API call. Entries without it are scored as before. An entry may also carry `must_not_claim` (added after the Codex review of b322d5d7 and two adversarial reviews): a phrase stated as fact fails. A negation (no, not, never, cannot, without, nor, nothing, none, neither, n't) excuses it only within the 12 words before it and inside its clause; a semicolon, dash, "but" or "and" ends the clause. So "it makes no claim that it can lower blood pressure" passes, while "it has no added sugar and can lower blood pressure" and "it isn't a medicine, but it keeps you regular" fail. Affirming idioms that hold a negation word ("no doubt", "not only", "no wonder", "no question", "without doubt", "none other than") never excuse a claim (Codex, range review of 2026-10-07). Markdown emphasis is stripped and curly apostrophes straightened. It is a heuristic, so the phrases stay claim-shaped. Referral checks ask for "your doctor" (so "Brad is a doctor" does not count) and disclosure checks ask for both "owns" and "profit", with "n't profit" and "not profit" forbidden.
- Usage signal (declared): no new event. Questions naming the drink show in the stored transcripts of the blog chat bubble, chatbot embed, Discord and YouTube (30-day window, US-15 AC8). The main widget stores no text (US-15 AC7), so its answers cannot be audited after the fact.
- Known limitations (orchestrator ruling, 2026-10-07: leave the router and posture files as they are):
  - Routing is not asserted. Router rule 7 names only MicroVitamin and Sleep as Brad's products, and on 2026-10-07 it sent most Potassium Fiber questions to the potassium, psyllium, kidney, hypertension and ACE-inhibitor entries. Each fixture pins the 3-run router intersection for its text (the router sees no surface), minus what production skips at the 120K article cap (US-15 AC23). A later change in what the router returns is not caught here.
  - The posture files name only MicroVitamin, MicroVitamin+ and Sleep. The drink's surface rules live in the products.md editor notes.
  - The doctor fixtures model the drstanfield.com web chat only. YouTube and Discord also use the doctor posture but add their platform prompt (YouTube caps a reply at 90 words), which the harness does not load; nor does it load the evidence document, the knowledge overview or user data, as before this story.
  - The limit-potassium fixture's "your doctor" can be met by echoing the user's own words.
  - Nothing reruns this category automatically when products.md or a posture file changes; run it by hand before such a deploy.
- Tests: ✅ 22 fixed-handle answer checks, category `product-potassium-fiber` in `tools/test-queries.json` (11 doctor, 11 brand; AC1 to AC5). Run: `npx tsx tools/test-chatbot-matching.ts --category product-potassium-fiber --fixed-handles-only --answer-check-runs 3`. 2026-10-07: 22/22 at 3 runs each (majority) on `products.md` at 8ad6a7bc, before the research rule and the medicine-spacing direction; the paid suite has not run since (the test workspace is out until 2026-11-01), so the research rule was checked live instead (see the chatbot changelog); 3/22 at 1 run on `products.md` before a97d629e (the three that pass are the guards that need the drink absent from the answer: two doctor-surface "never volunteer" questions and the brand kidney-disease "which product" question). ✅ `tools/test-chatbot-matching.test.ts` (AC6, offline: each answer call carries its own surface's posture, after products and before articles; a referral-free answer, a stated claim and a claim after an unrelated negation or an affirming idiom fail, and so does an outcome-research summary (the five AC3 fixtures forbid "lowered systolic", "systolic pressure by", "mmHg", "meta-analysis", "randomized trials", "randomised trials" and "Cochrane"), while denials pass; a liveness check confirms every other fixture fails on a stub answer).

## Epic F — Staying engaged

### US-17 · Email reminders — the retention engine (cloud lane BUILT 2026-08-13; typed lane = US-23)
As a user who has connected a cloud and entered real screening/blood data, I am reminded by email when something comes due **without having had to hunt for a setting** — because a reminder I never receive is the whole reason the tool failed to change my behaviour.

**Reframed 2026-08-14 (constitution):** reminders are not a settings feature — they are the product's retention engine, and email is the primary surface for the primary avatar. This story covers the provider-verified (cloud-connect) lane, which the funnel shows is the rare path; the typed-email lane — ~10× larger and therefore the trunk — is promoted to its own story, **US-23**. Shared mechanics that apply to BOTH lanes (full-schedule-in-every-email, the annual floor, calendar links) are specified in US-23 AC3/AC6 and US-24 to avoid duplication.

**Status: the opt-in design was a proven failure; the opt-out replacement is built (2026-08-13).** The old behaviour was explicit opt-in behind three barriers (cloud-connect → scroll past the disclaimer to the bottom of the results panel → two clicks). Result: **~1 genuine opt-in in two months** (the second `reminder_optin_v2` row is Brad's own e2e test). Nothing was broken — cron healthy, sends correct, nothing yet due (earliest 2027-05-12) — the feature simply never reached anyone.

**What now ships (`autoEnrolReminders`, [standalone/reminders.ts](../widget-src/standalone/reminders.ts)):** every app load on a reminders-capable cloud enrols the user unprompted, unless the file already records a decision. Details that are load-bearing, not incidental:

- **No credential, no popup (rebuilt 2026-09-10 — see AC7).** Auto-enrolment reads the account email in the browser (Drive: a signed ID token, which grants nothing; Dropbox: `get_current_account`; GitHub: `/user/emails`) and posts only that. It never opens a Google popup — it runs at page load, where a popup is both browser-blocked and unasked-for — and the popup path that used to send a Drive ACCESS token as proof is deleted. Failures are swallowed: an enrolment nobody asked for must not raise an error nobody asked for. Pinned by a real (unmocked) test on the adapter, `storage/drive-reminder-identity.test.ts`.
- **The disclosure renders ABOVE the widget, not inside the plan panel.** The plan is slide 2 of the mobile tab layout and every connect path reloads onto slide 1, so a notice inside it would announce itself to an off-screen panel; it is also absent from the no-data-yet render branch — exactly the user who just connected. It is the reminders control itself, so the off switch is one click from the sentence announcing it, and it survives a reload (sessionStorage) until dismissed.
- **Shopify surface only** (a deliberate narrowing of AC1's literal text — Brad to confirm). The Pages/self-host build is marketed as almost no Brad server (the record and the chat never reach us; Google sign-in, reminders and scrubbed Sentry errors do) and runs uploads + chat on the user's own key; enrolling that user unprompted would post their account email to Brad's server at page load, the one thing they chose that build to avoid. They keep the manual toggle. It also matters for the kill criterion: `trackProductEvent` no-ops off-Shopify, so a Pages opt-out would be invisible to the very ratio this decision reverts on.
- **"No email to give" stops the retry loop permanently** (`hr_reminders_autoenrol_blocked`): a GitHub token without the email permission, or a pre-openid Drive grant, will answer the same way next visit. The manual toggle still works and asks the user to type the address once (AC9) — never a silent enrolment on a typed address. Transient failures are not blocks.
- **Switching clouds moves the enrolment, it doesn't duplicate it.** Server rows are keyed by email, so a fresh opt-in cannot replace one made under a different provider; the old row is cancelled first. Not counted as an opt-out — a storage switch is not a user's verdict on reminders.
- **Erasing your data reaches the server row** (AC1b — tombstoned, or deleted when Drive can prove the inbox), via a pre-erase hook — the capability token that authorises the delete dies with the file, so it cannot run afterwards.

**The decision (Brad, 2026-08-11):** move to reminders **on by default**, opt-out rather than opt-in. Sends are rare by design (screening 90d / bloods 180d / med-review 365d cooldowns), so the cost to a user who ignores them is near zero while the value to one who forgets a colonoscopy is the product's entire thesis.

**Two constraints that bound the redesign** (they are not negotiable and any implementation must satisfy both):
1. **RETIRED for Dropbox, GitHub and typed on 2026-09-10 (Brad; see AC7–AC10). Drive alone keeps the provider's word, via the ID token.** Former text: The email must be verified — the provider's word, or proven delivery (superseded 2026-08-14; original wording said "never a client-typed address … only after a confirm-link round trip").** The threat is unchanged: anyone can enter `victim@example.com` and make Brad's server email a stranger. What changed is the accepted proof: Brad rejected the confirm click (loses 20–40% of honest users) in favour of **delivery-validation + defense-in-depth**, now specified in US-23 — bounce/complaint webhook un-enrols, 3-day quiet period before any reminder, server-side label allow-list (no attacker-authored text can transit), typed writes can never touch a provider-verified row, refreshes preserve token + cooldowns (no resend amplification, no broken unsubscribe links), one plan-ready email per address ever, prominent in-body unsubscribe. A typed address with none of those protections remains forbidden.
2. **Default-on must stay visible, never silent.** Auto-enrolling silently would push every cloud-connected user's due-dates + labels to Brad's server without them knowing — quietly weakening the "your health data lives in your cloud, not our servers" promise that the product is marketed on ([architecture-v2.html](architecture-v2.html)), and inviting spam complaints that damage the sending domain shared with transactional mail. **Recommended design point:** at the cloud-connect success moment, a visible, pre-checked "Email me when something comes due" with a one-line plain statement of exactly what leaves the device (due date + label + email — never values), unmissable on one screen, plus a permanent off switch in settings.

**Where the audience actually is (live pull, 2026-08-10 — this reorders the work):** Klaviyo holds **874 captured emails** on the commerce list (+39 edu), still growing ~4–5/day (135 in the last 30d). `cloud_connect_success` has fired **2 times ever**. So the provider-verified lane covers ~2 people and the typed-address lane covers ~900: **the typed lane is not a nice-to-have Phase 2, it is the entire audience.** Build order follows the numbers, not the ease.

**Confirmation email sends via Resend, never Klaviyo (decision 2026-08-11).** Reminder consent must never be coupled to the marketing list: Klaviyo suppression is list-wide, so a supplement-promo unsubscribe would silently stop "your colonoscopy is due" — a marketing preference overriding a health reminder, with no error and nobody noticing. Two consents, two systems, two suppression lists. Supporting reasons: the confirm mail is textbook transactional (one-time, user-triggered, expected in seconds); a confirm landing in spam fails the whole redesign silently, and transactional streams land better; Resend is already wired (`reminder-v2.server.ts`, `email.server.ts`). Precedent already in the code — [api.reminders-v2.ts](../app/routes/api.reminders-v2.ts) takes `verified.email` for reminders and a SEPARATE `marketingEmail` for Klaviyo, fire-and-forget so Klaviyo can never fail an opt-in. Bonus: confirming before adding to Klaviyo filters typos/fakes, which raises Meta/Google audience match rates and cuts per-contact spend.

**No confirm-click gate (Brad, 2026-08-11; reaffirmed 2026-09-10 when the credential proof was dropped — the confirm-link alternative was planned, reviewed and rejected for the same reason).** Double opt-in loses 20–40% of legitimate signups — taxing every honest user to catch a rare bad one. Instead the plan-ready email (US-22) does the work: a bounce proves the address is dead, and delivery is the enrollment trigger. The residual risk a bounce can't catch is a typo into a *valid stranger's* address (`john@` for `johnn@`), which delivers fine; AC2c bounds that (rare sends + prominent one-click unsubscribe). A click on that email is a strong ownership signal but is never required — treat clicks, not opens, as real (Apple Mail Privacy Protection fakes opens).

**The ~900 existing captures (still needs a Brad call):** they gave an email for a report, not for reminders, and predate any send. A bulk "want reminders?" mail is a re-permission campaign to people who never asked — real complaint risk on a domain that also carries transactional mail. Preferred shape if we do it: one genuinely useful email (plan re-entry + the reminders offer), once. Both lists are `single_opt_in` today, so no confirmation habit exists with these contacts.

- AC1: On cloud-connect success (Drive/Dropbox/GitHub), enrollment happens **immediately and without any user action** — true opt-out (Brad, 2026-08-11). A user who reads the line and clicks away IS enrolled; only an explicit toggle-off unenrolls. The disclosure is a visible statement + already-on switch on that same screen, never a gate.
- AC1b: Consequence of AC1, accepted knowingly: every cloud-connecting user's due date + label + verified email reaches the server, where before only explicit opt-ins did. Still no measurement, lab value, or reasoning — the local-first invariant holds — but the population sending metadata widens from ~2 to everyone who connects. **Amended 2026-09-10 (review of aae35a3):** a toggle-off or erase TOMBSTONES the row (schedule emptied; email + token kept) on every lane; it is DELETED outright only when the request also carries a valid Google ID token for the row's own address. Reason: a bare-token delete made optin→cancel→optin mint a fresh `isNew` — and a fresh plan-ready email — every cycle. Consequence, stated plainly: for Dropbox/GitHub/typed users "delete all my data" leaves their address in a tombstone on Brad's server until they unsubscribe from an email. **Bounded 2026-09-10:** a row switched off stays 90 days, then is deleted — the daily reminder cron sweeps emptied rows older than `TOMBSTONE_TTL_DAYS` (`purgeTombstones` in `app/lib/reminder-v2.server.ts`), after the day's sends, and a purge failure is reported to Sentry rather than failing the run. Residual: past the window a replayed optin on that address is `isNew` again and earns one more plan-ready email — at most one per address per 90 days, under the capture route's 5/day-per-email limiter. Tests: `app/lib/reminder-v2-typed.test.ts` (window, live rows untouched, select failure throws), `app/lib/reminder-v2-cron.server.test.ts` (once per run; a purge error never fails the day).
- AC2 (amended 2026-09-10): The enrolled email is either Google-verified (ID token) OR an address whose plan-ready email **delivered without bouncing** — that now covers Dropbox, GitHub and typed alike — the typed lane is now its own story, **US-23** (promoted 2026-08-14: it is ~10× the cloud lane and the highest-leverage unbuilt thing in the product). No confirm-click gate — see the consent reasoning below.
- AC2b: A hard bounce disenrolls: no reminders, and the address is suppressed (unsubscribed, not deleted) in Klaviyo — matching US-22 AC3's reversibility decision.
- AC2c (widened 2026-09-10): Because a delivered-but-mistyped address belongs to a stranger, EVERY reminder to EVERY lane carries the one-click unsubscribe prominently in the body, not just the header. Rare sends (90/180/365d) + one click to stop = bounded, self-correcting harm.
- AC3: A user can turn reminders off permanently in one action, from the results surface and from any reminder email (RFC 8058 one-click unsubscribe already ships).
- **Known residual risks, accepted not fixed (adversarial review 2026-08-13).** (1) If a user moves their `health-roadmap.json` to the Drive trash, the app sees a legitimately-empty query result — indistinguishable from a new user — and re-enrols, minting a token that invalidates the one in the real file; the next schedule push 404s and flips them to `cancelled`. A silent unsubscribe, very narrow. (2) Drive does two `tryServerRefresh` round trips per load until enrolment succeeds (one in `resolveBackend`, one in `getReminderIdToken`), on two adapter instances sharing localStorage. Refresh grants don't re-issue the refresh token, so no credential can be stranded — it's waste, not risk.
- AC4: Turning reminders off survives a sync from a second device (see the 2026-08-07 incident below — this AC exists because that exact bug shipped).
- AC5: The server still receives only due date + label + verified email + capability token. No measurement, lab value, or reasoning ever leaves the device — default-on must not widen the data sent. **Open nuance for Brad (adversarial review, 2026-08-13):** a label can carry an inference even though it carries no value. `'Lung screening (low-dose CT)'` is emitted ONLY for a ≥15-pack-year smoker, `'Medication review'` only for someone on active medication, and result-dependent intervals make some `dueAt` values a function of a result. Under opt-in the user chose that knowingly; under default-on they didn't. The shipped copy was changed to be concrete rather than reassuring ("the NAME and due date of each check-up, for example 'Colonoscopy — due May 2027'") instead of the earlier "never your measurements or results", which was true and misleading. If Brad wants the inference closed rather than disclosed, the options are generic labels ("A screening is due") or dropping the lung item.
- AC6: Local-storage-only users (no cloud, no account email) are never enrolled and are never shown a broken control.
- AC7 (Brad, 2026-09-10 — a ChatGPT audit found enrolment posting the Dropbox access token and the GitHub PAT as "proof of email"; a PAT confers write access to the repo the health data lives in): **No storage credential ever leaves the browser for reminders.** The optin request carries the account email + the schedule (+ the optional typed marketing address). Google Drive alone still sends its signed ID token — it grants nothing, and the server reads the verified address from it. `api.google-token` is unchanged (Drive connect needs it; its `idToken` passthrough now also feeds the browser-side email prefill).
- AC8: **An address-only optin follows the typed lane's rules, for every lane.** A NEW address creates the row, receives its capability token ONCE, gets the plan-ready email (US-22) and sits out the 3-day quiet period. An EXISTING address is a schedule refresh: provider immutable, token never rotated and **never returned on the ADDRESS lane** (returning it there would hand the cancel capability to whoever typed the address — the silent-unsubscribe attack). **Scoped 2026-09-10:** the google-drive lane ALWAYS receives its token, new row or existing, because the caller proved the inbox with a signed ID token; withholding it stranded every Drive user on a second device and every user whose squatted row had just been rotated (no token in the file means schedule pushes never start and the toggle-off is a no-op). An address-lane row's token still rotates when a Google-verified caller proves that address. Email-link unsubscribe is a tombstone for every lane, and a tombstoned token refuses schedule pushes so the device flips its own file to cancelled. **Accepted, plainly: the refreshed-vs-new reply is a bounded membership oracle** — an unauthenticated optin reveals whether an address is enrolled. Bounded by 5/day per email and 20/hour per hashed IP (the capture route's numbers); accepted by Brad 2026-09-10 over the confirm-click alternative. **Residual (review of aae35a3):** after an email-link unsubscribe, an address-only optin naming that address refills the schedule silently (no plan-ready email, since it is not `isNew`), and the widget offers that inbox no off switch other than the link in the next reminder. **Token squatting is closed:** a Google-verified optin ROTATES the token when the existing row was not itself Google-verified (`provider` = `'google-drive'` means verified; the widget's no-ID-token Drive fallback enrols as `'typed'`). **Per-email limiter key** is normalised (case, `+tag`, Gmail dots) — the stored address is not. **Transient Drive refresh failures never set the "no email" block**; a fresh connect clears it.
- **Lane B note, deferred 2026-09-10 (late): the tombstone refill.** AC8's residual is that an address-lane optin naming a tombstoned address refills that row's schedule silently. The better answer is to send the owner a mail instead: an address-lane optin that hits a tombstone emails that address a "turn reminders back on" link carrying the row's own token, so the person who owns the inbox decides, and nobody else can restart a schedule by typing their address. Not built. It has no usage signal yet — we have never observed the case — and per the constitution a feature we cannot evaluate does not get built.
- AC11 (2026-09-10): A `google-drive` optin without an ID token is REFUSED with a 400 by the request schema, never downgraded to the address lane. A body naming the verified provider must carry the proof that provider's lane rests on; accepting the address instead would let anyone write a row wearing the verified label. Before this AC (deployed c2d4192, the morning of 2026-09-10) the address lane accepted that body; a row it wrote would have kept a squatter's token across the owner's verification. Checked the same day: every `google-drive` row (9) predates that deploy, so no such row exists and nothing is rotated. Two stated residuals on the verified lane: an existing verified row re-enrolled from a new device now shows that device the one-time "reminders set up" notice, and a Drive device whose file records no decision refills an email-link tombstone on its next visit (it always did; the email's off link works every time).
- AC9: When the provider has no email to give (a GitHub token without "Email addresses: read-only"; a pre-openid Drive grant), auto-enrolment stops and the control asks for the address once, visibly — never a silent enrolment on a typed address. An address already enrolled elsewhere shows "already set up for x@…; use the link in any reminder email to turn them off", never a broken "on".
- AC10: `reminder_optin` is counted by the SERVER when a new row lands, for every lane (it is in `SERVER_ONLY_EVENT_NAMES`; the client emission is deleted). Only the server knows the enrolment landed, and abuse enrolments count rather than hide — the ratio stays honest. `reminder_optout` is unchanged.
- **Usage signal (Lane B, declare before code):** `reminder_optin` fires on enrolment (auto or manual; server-side since 2026-09-10, AC10) and `reminder_optout` on disable — both wired 2026-08-13, both in the shared `PRODUCT_EVENT_NAMES` enum. `reminder_sent` fires server-side on each successful reminder send (nil-UUID sentinel; metadata = provider + due-item count, never labels or addresses) — added 2026-08-30 (W35 backlog #3, Brad-authorized live) after the first real send proved sends were invisible. Server-only event names and the nil sentinel are rejected at the client event route, so a browser cannot forge these counters (adversarial review, same day — closed the pre-existing `report_email_*` hole too). The ratio is the honest measure. Kill criterion: if opt-out exceeds ~30% of enrolments, or spam complaints appear in Resend, the default-on decision is wrong and reverts. Caveat to read the first numbers with: `trackProductEvent` is Shopify-surface-only and throttles to once per event name per tab session, so these are session counters, not per-user counts.
- Evidence: ~1 genuine opt-in ever (live query 2026-08-10); `reminder_optin` 0 fires since instrumentation went live 2026-08-06 — but both existing opt-ins PREDATE instrumentation, so zero events measures reach, not rejection. First default-on week (2026-W33): 18 enrolments, 0 opt-outs (kill-ratio 0% vs the 30% threshold); `reminder_optin_v2` 2 → 20 rows all-time. Week 2 (2026-W34): 38 enrolments (+111%), still 0 opt-outs ever (0/56 cumulative); rows 58 all-time (52 typed + 6 google-drive, ~9:1). Week 3 (2026-W35): 31 enrolments, still 0 opt-outs (0/87 cumulative); rows 89 (81 typed + 8 drive, ~10:1); 0 bounces, 0 complaints. **The first genuine reminder send happened 2026-08-28** (W35): a row enrolled 08-25 with an item already due 08-01 carries a `last_sent` stamp — the typed lane enrols overdue-at-capture items, so "nothing due until 2027" is stale; sends are live now (evidenced by the cron stamp; no send event existed at the time — `reminder_sent` shipped 2026-08-30, W35 backlog #3). Cron `reminder_v2_cron` healthy (lock acquired daily; a transient-lock-error day-skip class was hardened 08-28 — see US-28). Week 4 (2026-W36): 20 enrolments and the **first opt-out ever** (kill-ratio 1/109 cumulative vs the 30% threshold — `reminder_optout`'s emit is now production-verified); rows 109 all-time (101 typed + 7 drive + the first dropbox row); `reminder_sent` fired twice (typed, 08-31 colorectal + 09-01 breast) and matched the window's only two `last_sent` stamps exactly — the event and the stamp now corroborate each other, so sends are observable (W35 backlog #3 closed). Weeks 5–6 (2026-W37/W38): 26 + 14 enrolments; second opt-out ever (2/149); rows 149 (136 typed + 11 drive + 2 dropbox); `reminder_sent` 2 in W37 (matching the two `last_sent` map dates) and 0 in W38 (no date in the window — corroborated). Week 7 (2026-W39): 47 enrolments (46 rows: 42 typed / 3 drive / 1 dropbox; 195 all-time), 0 opt-outs (2/195); `reminder_sent` 3 (09-22/24/25) = 3 `last_sent` dates, one-to-one.
- Tests: ✅ schedule/due logic + store persistence (2026-08-07); ✅ enrolment path — rebuilt 2026-09-10 for AC7–AC10: `standalone/reminders.test.ts` (25 — AC1 auto-enrol/token, AC7 Google sends only the ID token and Dropbox/GitHub only the address, AC8 refreshed → no file write and no notice, AC9 no-email blocks / transient retries / `ReminderEmailNeeded` / typed address, AC10 no client `reminder_optin`, AC4, AC6, Pages no-op, cloud switch, erase teardown, `reminder_optout` only on a server-confirmed cancel), `storage/drive-reminder-identity.test.ts` (4 — the real adapter opens no popup, decodes and persists the ID-token email), `app/lib/reminder-v2-typed.test.ts` (16 — the silent-unsubscribe attack returns no token and rotates nothing, the tombstone refill is not isNew, tombstoned tokens refuse pushes, every-lane quiet period + tombstone), `api.measurements.test.ts` (7 — `enrolByEmail` refresh sends nothing), `storage/roadmap-store-data-safety.test.ts` (2 — an opt-out survives an erase; no token or address survives it). **What they do NOT prove:** reminders.test.ts mocks at the module boundary, so it pins the fetch payload and the branch logic, not that a real user gets enrolled. ✅ send observability (2026-08-30): `reminder-v2-cron.server.test.ts` pins `reminder_sent` on send success and its absence on send failure. ❌ still manual: the live cloud-connect → enrol → notice-visible journey on real WebKit (needs a real provider round trip).
- **Incident 2026-08-07:** store-level tests found `setGlobalReminderOptout` mutated rows without bumping their merge stamps — "turn off all reminders" silently reverted on the next sync. Fixed same day (routed through the stamped upsert path); regression test pins it. **The "load-bearing TODO" it left is resolved (2026-08-13) — partly by evidence, partly by code.** By evidence: in the v2 builds the off switch is `cancelReminders()`, which tombstones the server row (it deletes it outright only when the call carries a Google ID token for that address — AC1b) and flips the file's opt-in to `cancelled`, a stamped LWW singleton that carries the opt-out to every device; the per-category `reminderPreferences` UI that `setGlobalReminderOptout` drives (`ReminderSettings` in ResultsPanel) is unreachable in both v2 builds, because `standalone/app.tsx` is the only renderer of `HealthTool` and always passes `remindersSection`, which wins that branch.
