Nothing needed seeding. **By code, because the first draft of this feature got AC4 wrong and an adversarial review caught it before it shipped:** "Delete all my data" resets the file, and an empty file reads as "never decided" — so the next load would have re-enrolled the user, undoing an explicit opt-out via a button promising the opposite, on every device at once (a higher `eraseEpoch` wins the merge wholesale, so a second device's `cancelled` record couldn't save them either). The erase now carries the decision without the identity: `status:'cancelled'`, no token, no email. Same bug class as 2026-08-07 — an off switch that comes back on — which is why AC4 is stated as a survival property, not a UI feature. Separately, the dead v1 preferences chain is now unreachable code awaiting a deletion sweep (spans HealthTool + ResultsPanel + api.ts + roadmap-store; it predates this change, so it is a follow-up commit rather than a rider on a consent-model change).

### US-18 · Generate my plan as a downloadable PDF (guest capture button)
As a guest, I enter my email and **immediately get my whole plan as a downloadable/printable PDF** — no account, no waiting on an inbox. (Corrected 2026-08-11: this story used to claim "I can email myself the report", which has not been true since `525e6be`, 2026-06-12 — "NO Resend report email, and NO health data reaches the server". Nothing was ever emailed. The general print/save path is US-08.)
- AC1: The button renders the plan and opens the save-as-PDF/print window **client-side** — the plan never leaves the device.
- AC2: The typed address is subscribed to Klaviyo fire-and-forget; a Klaviyo failure never blocks or delays the PDF.
- AC3: Rate-limited per address (guest report limit) and behind app-proxy HMAC; Shopify surface only — the Pages build has no Brad-server capture path and must no-op.
- AC4: The PDF is the delivery. US-22 has shipped, so the UI may promise the plan-ready email; once US-23 ships, the capture UI must also carry its reminders disclosure (US-23 AC4).
- AC5 (2026-09-10): Klaviyo receives an email address and nothing else. `KlaviyoProfileData` still declared sex, height, weight and birth fields, and the whole profile-properties step (create, 409, PATCH) was still in the code, with no caller filling them since the v1 teardown — dead code a skeptic screenshots before reading the callers. The fields and the step are deleted, so `subscribeToKlaviyo` sends the address alone, which is what it has actually done since June. The 394 live profiles still carrying those properties from v1 were scrubbed on 2026-09-10.
- AC6 (2026-10-04, Brad; adversarial review of the product-health W40 plan): a refused print window is never silent. `openPrintWindow` returns `false` when `window.open` gives `null`, which mail and social apps' own browsers often do, and both callers ignored it: the capture marked the record captured and hid the email box with no PDF and no word. Now, when the window is refused, the capture still completes as before (the address is captured, the plan-ready email goes out, and the plan was already saved on this device as it was typed), and the person is told so beside Save as PDF, in words Brad signs: "The PDF window didn't open. Try Save as PDF again. Some apps' built-in browsers block it." It makes no claim that the plan is saved here: a device that refused storage runs from memory (US-09 AC16), and nothing tells the panel. Every Save as PDF button says the same when its own window is refused, beside the button pressed (the header, and on the Pages build the bottom one too), and each refusal is announced again. A capture whose report could not be built (`getReportHtml` failing) counts as a refusal, and so does a refusal on a capture that then fails to send, shown beside that error. A later window that opens clears the message, and so does an erase. Nothing sends them to another browser, which would not hold this plan. On a phone the Plan tab's height follows the message (the Swiper's auto-height is re-measured). Usage signal: `pdf_window_blocked` (browser, name only), once per tab session. Known limit: a window that opens but whose browser ignores `print()` cannot be detected.
- Evidence: 874 addresses captured on the commerce Klaviyo list, ~4–5/day, all `single_opt_in`; 50 unsubscribed lifetime.
- Tests: 🟡 copy + captured-flag tested; PDF path manual (shares US-08's untested print pipeline). AC6 (2026-10-04): ✅ `ResultsPanel.skip-gate.test.tsx` (12 AC6 cases: exact copy, capture completes on a refusal, re-announce, Swiper re-measure, refusal beside a send error, report not built, erase, the Pages bottom button) + `product-events.server.test.ts`; ✅ Chromium render at 393px on the Pages build (slide height follows the note); ❌ owed after deploy: real WebKit on an iPhone viewport, and one real in-app browser. Historical note: a guest's report email silently failed in v1 (feedback 2026-03-16) — that v1 path is gone.

### US-44 · Funnel page: Save as PDF without the email box — **built 2026-10-02 (Brad-approved 2026-10-02), live 2026-10-03: code health-roadmap-831/edu-97 (bdf4c160), AC3 sign-up marking health-roadmap-832/edu-98 (103adea1); setting ticked on `/pages/start` (template `page.health-start.json`) only**
As someone who just gave my email on the `/pages/health` landing page, I reach `drstanfield.com/pages/start` and save my plan as a PDF without being asked for my email again. I can still ask for check-up reminders, but nothing makes me. Plan: claude_business `docs/drstanfield-popup/youtube-roadmap-lead-magnet-plan.md` §1 items 4–5.
- AC1: The app block has a `skip_email_gate` checkbox ("Skip the email box before Save as PDF (funnel pages only)", default off), emitted on `#health-tool-root` as `data-skip-email-gate`. Only the literal `true` turns it on (`resolveSkipEmailGate`). The homepage and `/pages/roadmap` blocks carry no settings, so they stay gated.
- AC2: With it on, the plan header shows "Save as PDF" from the first render and no email box gates it. The skip is a property of the page: it never calls `markReportEmailCaptured()` and writes nothing to the user's record, so another device or page behaves as its own block says.
- AC3: Reminders stay available as one optional line, "Email me when a check-up is due", with a helper line saying the PDF does not need it. Submitting runs the same capture call as US-23 AC1 (`sendGuestReport` → `klaviyoCapture { email, schedule }`), opens no print window and counts no A/B conversion. A successful sign-up calls `markReportEmailCaptured()` (Brad, 2026-10-03), so the plan-ready and reminder emails' link back to `/pages/roadmap` doesn't ask for the email again in the same browser, or on any device using the same connected cloud (another browser starts empty, US-22 AC13). On the next render the page shows the captured view. The bare page skip (AC2) still persists nothing. A failure keeps the field, shows the error and marks nothing.
- AC4: The line carries the US-23 AC11 disclosure, worded for a sign-up ("Signing up adds you to the **MicroVitamin mailing list**…") because getting the plan no longer adds anyone. The US-09 AC5 storage sentence stays last in the box. The line renders once, at the top of the plan, without the `guestEmail` id or the stage-3 highlight, so finishing the vitals never pulls focus to an optional field.
- AC5: With the setting off, behaviour is unchanged: the email box gates the PDF, prints first, then marks the record captured. A visitor whose record is already captured sees today's captured view on either page.
- AC6 (2026-10-04, found while building US-18 AC6): Save as PDF shows on a phone. The plan's header holds the ungated Save as PDF on the Shopify surface (captured, or gate skipped), and `styles.css` hid the whole column header at 768px and below (the tabs name the columns there), so on a phone `/pages/start` offered no way to save the PDF at all and `/pages/roadmap` offered none after the capture's own print. The live check of 2026-10-03 was desktop only. Now the plan column's header keeps its actions on a phone (title and step stay hidden), so Save as PDF and the US-18 AC6 message sit at the top of the Plan tab, as on desktop. The Pages build's plan header shows on a phone too (its Save as PDF was already ungated; the bottom button stays). The input column's header stays hidden on a phone (its guide link has the US-38 footer entry). Verify on a real iPhone viewport after deploy.
- **Usage signal (Brad, 2026-10-03):** sign-ups on the `/pages/health` Klaviyo form (list `WaCdqF`) are the measure of funnel volume; US-44's own use (PDF saves without the gate, Remind-me sign-ups) is not separately measured, by decision. No new product event. `reminder_optin {provider:'typed'}` still counts this line's enrolments without a page split.
- **Klaviyo (Brad, 2026-10-03, decided):** funnel opt-ins go to list `TpwCKK` like every other capture, and so receive flow `TYaNR2` ("Quick question about your Health Plan") a day later, unless already on `TpwCKK`. No source marker; US-18 AC5 unchanged.
- Tests: ✅ `ResultsPanel.skip-gate.test.tsx` (11: AC1 schema + root attribute, AC2 no persistence + ungated print, AC3 enrolment/failure/validation, AC4 disclosure + single box, AC5 the gated path); `assistant-config.test.ts` (AC1 parsing). ✅ Live check 2026-10-03 (desktop WebKit, sex + height entered): `/pages/start` shows Save as PDF and the reminders line with no gate (`data-skip-email-gate="true"`); `/pages/roadmap` keeps the gate (`"false"`). ✅ Dropbox and Google connect on `/pages/start` (2026-10-03, after Brad registered the redirect URI in both consoles): each reaches its sign-in page with `redirect_uri=https://drstanfield.com/pages/start` and no mismatch error. AC6 (2026-10-04): ✅ `ResultsPanel.skip-gate.test.tsx` now 28 (4 AC6: the plan header carries the phone modifier only when it has actions, the placeholder and input headers never, and a styles.css check of the 768px rule); ✅ Chromium render at 393px (Save as PDF at the top of the Plan tab, page width 393); ❌ owed after deploy: real WebKit on an iPhone viewport of `/pages/start` and `/pages/roadmap`.

### US-22 · Plan-ready email + address validation — **NEW BUILD, Brad-approved 2026-08-11**
As a guest who handed over my email, I **receive an email** confirming my plan is ready with a link back to it — so I have a way back in, and so a dead address is detectable.

**Why:** ~900 people typed an email expecting something and got nothing (US-18). Those addresses have never been validated, and the tool has no re-entry path. This email is also what makes reminders possible (US-17 AC2): delivery — not a confirm click — is the enrollment trigger.

**The old implementation CANNOT be restored.** Pre-teardown `sendReportEmail(userId, client)` read health values out of Supabase and built an email containing waist, BP, HbA1c, LDL, ApoB, Lp(a) (`git show bbd79fd^:app/lib/email.server.ts`). Those tables are purged, and that design violates the v2 invariant — no health value may transit Brad's server. Lift its layout/markup for reference only.

**The v2 shape carries NO health data** (Brad, agreed 2026-08-11): it confirms the plan is ready, links back into the tool (where the plan re-renders from the user's own localStorage/cloud), and states what reminders they'll get. Local-first promise intact, and it still yields both signals we need — bounce (dead address) and click (ownership + re-engagement). The health specifics still reach people via the reminders themselves, which are already permitted to carry a label and a due date.

- AC1: On capture, Resend sends a plan-ready email containing no measurement, lab value, medication, or screening data — only the re-entry link and what reminders to expect.
- AC2: The send never blocks the user's plan; the US-18 PDF delivery is unchanged and a Resend failure is logged, not surfaced.
- AC3: A Resend **bounce webhook** (new route — none exists today, signature-verified) marks the address dead → **suppressed/unsubscribed in Klaviyo, not deleted** (Brad, 2026-08-11: keeps the record and stays reversible if a bounce is misclassified) and never enrolled in reminders (US-17 AC2b).
- AC4: A delivered (non-bounced) address stays in Klaviyo and becomes reminder-eligible — delivery is the trigger, no confirm click.
- AC5: The re-entry link is click-tracked; **clicks, not opens**, are the ownership signal (Apple Mail Privacy Protection fakes opens).
- AC6: Shopify surface only; the Pages build no-ops.
- AC7: Once shipped, US-18's UI may promise the email (AC4 there).
- AC8: **New captures only** (Brad, 2026-08-11) — no backfill send to the ~900 pre-existing addresses. They stay unvalidated and reminder-ineligible until Brad revisits, once real bounce/complaint rates from new sends are known.
- AC9: Ships on **both** Fly apps (commerce + education) together; they share one Resend key and from-address. The bounce webhook needs only ONE endpoint, hosted on commerce: both apps send through the same Resend account, and the handler writes to the shared Supabase + the single commerce Klaviyo list.
- AC10: The webhook also handles `email.complained` (spam complaint), not just `email.bounced` — under an opt-out model a complaint is the loudest possible "I never wanted this", and must suppress in Klaviyo AND unenroll from reminders immediately.
- **Usage signal:** `report_email_sent` / `report_email_bounced` / `report_email_clicked`. Bounce rate on new captures is also the estimate for how much of the legacy ~900 is junk — the input to the backfill decision. First live week (2026-W33): 25 sent, 10 clicked (40% CTR), 1 bounced (4%, webhook un-enrolled it end-to-end in production), 0 complaints. 2026-W34: 35 / 21 / 0; 2026-W35: 29 / 10 / 0; 2026-W36: 19 / 11 / 0 (sent / click events / bounced; 0 complaints ever). Standing caveat from the product-health loop (W34): the email events carry one constant `visitor_id` and no per-recipient dimension, so click:send is an EVENT ratio, never a CTR — the all-time bounce count was 1 at W34 (2 by W39). 2026-W37: 24 / 14 / 0; 2026-W38: 13 / 8 / 0. 2026-W39: 47 / 26 / 1; 2026-W40: 19 / 17 / 0.
- **Brad prerequisite: ✅ DONE 2026-08-13.** Webhook registered in Resend (bounced + complained → commerce route), `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set via file→fly→shred. Live-verified end-to-end with a REAL bounce: signature accepted, `{"ok":true,"action":"bounced","count":1}`, Klaviyo suppressed, row deleted.
- **AC1 amended 2026-08-14 (US-23/US-24):** the email may now ALSO carry the reminder calendar — labels + due dates with add-to-calendar links — when the capture enrolled reminders. That is the constitution's permitted footprint and US-22 AC1's own "what reminders they'll expect", not health data; the "no measurement, lab value, medication, or screening RESULT" clause stands.
- AC11 (2026-09-10, late): a provider error names no recipient. Every Resend failure was thrown with the address it had just tried interpolated into the message, and that message ends at `console.error` and, on Fly, in stdout, which is unscrubbed: an outage would have written the enrolled list into the log a line at a time. The message now names no address of ours, and Resend's own text, which quotes the address back ("Invalid `to` field: …"), goes through `scrubText` before it is repeated. Applies to the plan-ready email, the reminders and the feedback path alike (`resendErrorText` in `app/lib/email.server.ts`). Tests: `app/lib/email-error-scrub.test.ts`
- AC12 (2026-09-24): the email says where THIS reader's plan lives, true for each lane. The guest copy (the capture button, `api.measurements`) says the plan is saved in the browser they made it in, and in the PDF if they saved one; that another phone, computer or browser starts empty; that Safari can clear a saved plan after a week without a visit; and that connecting Google Drive or Dropbox from that browser puts it on any device. The cloud copy (the reminders opt-in, `api.reminders-v2`, which needs a connected cloud) says the plan is in their own cloud storage and loads wherever they connect the same account. Each send site passes its lane, and the lane is a required argument, so a new caller cannot default into the wrong claim. The button reads "Open the Health Roadmap": it opens the tool and cannot promise the plan. The footer says what the server keeps ("Dr Brad's server keeps your reminder calendar and this email address. Your plan is not stored there.") instead of "Your health data lives only on your device or in your own cloud storage". No em dashes (docs/writing-style.md). Why: the old copy, "Your plan reloads from your own device or your own cloud storage", promised every guest a way back that works only in the one browser that made the plan. Darren made his in Safari, followed a reminder 7d 23h later, and met a blank form (2026-09-24); of 73 arrivals from the plan-ready email, 19 showed no plan within three minutes. Residual, accepted: a cloud user who types a second, NEW address into the capture box gets the guest copy, because that route cannot know the lane without trusting the client.
- AC13 (2026-09-24): an arrival from either email that finds this browser empty is told why. Both buttons go through `/roadmap/open`, which lands on `https://drstanfield.com/pages/roadmap?from=email`, a second hardcoded literal (`EMAIL_LANDING_URL`); the bare `ROADMAP_URL` stays for calendar text (US-24 AC2), and nothing in the click's query reaches `Location`. The widget reads the flag before its first await and strips it at once, keeping every other parameter byte for byte, the hash, and `history.state`. Only a guest session (no cloud, no reconnect) whose record is empty by HealthTool's own load test (`hasSavedRecord`) sees a notice above the tabs: "Looking for your plan? If you saved it to Google Drive or Dropbox, connect it and it loads. If not, it stays only in the browser you made it in, and Safari can clear that after a week without a visit. Your PDF still has it, if you saved one." Connect opens the storage picker in one click; Dismiss closes it. The check is awaited before the widget mounts, so a record read that fails shows no notice instead of rejecting into an empty mount (US-09 AC16; the adversarial review of 2026-09-25 found the await uncaught, and the notice's PDF line unhedged against the email's). A cloud or reconnect session, a record with data, and a missing or different `from` value all show nothing. On an iPhone 15 the whole notice is on screen at load, without scrolling or switching tabs.
- **Usage signal (AC13, declared 2026-09-24):** `email_landing_empty`, browser, name only, fired when the notice shows. Recovery is the same `visitor_id` then firing `results_viewed` (they re-entered) or `cloud_connect_success` (they found the plan in a cloud). Read it with `trackProductEvent`'s caveat: once per event name per tab session. Evidence (product-health): W39 2 rows, 1 visitor, no recovery (a Dropbox connect on 09-27 with no `results_viewed` after it); W40 14 rows from 8 visitors against 17 plan-ready clicks, 14 of the 17 clicks followed by an empty landing within ~3.4 s (16 of 21 since the 09-24 deploy), 1 of 8 visitors clearly recovered (connect, then `results_viewed`).
- AC14 (2026-10-04, Brad; product-health W40): the AC13 notice first tells a reader in the wrong browser what works. Evidence (09-24 to 10-03): all 16 empty landings came from 8 visitor ids that had never fired an event before, so each was a storage area that never held a plan (most likely the mail app's own browser), while 3 clicks that opened the person's own browser found the plan intact, one after 7 days; one capturer's own browser showed the plan 2 min 49 s after three empty taps. The notice now opens with "Opened this from your email app? Your plan is in the browser where you made it. Open drstanfield.com/pages/roadmap there." The address in that sentence is the copy target: Copy link writes the literal `https://drstanfield.com/pages/roadmap` (never `location.href`) and sits in the Connect and Dismiss row, so the notice grows by one paragraph only and still fits an iPhone 15 screen at load (AC13). When the clipboard is refused, the address in the sentence is selected and the button says to copy it by hand. The copy call runs inside the press, before any await. The cloud sentence and Connect stay as they are. No user-agent sniffing. Usage signal: `email_landing_link_copied` (browser, name only) on a successful copy. The recovery that follows happens under another visitor id, so it counts intent only.
- Tests: ✅ 18 (2026-08-13/14): `resend-webhook.server.test.ts` (12 — signature verify, bounce/complaint parse, permanence filter), `plan-ready-email.test.ts` (6 — no-health-fields shape, calendar section, links). 2026-09-24 (AC12/AC13, US-23 AC10): `plan-ready-email.test.ts` (26 — per-lane copy, lane required, footer, no em dash, reminder button and footer), `roadmap.open.test.ts` (12 — both literals, both counters, hostile queries never reach `Location`), `api.measurements.test.ts` + `api.reminders-v2.test.ts` (each send site's lane), `widget-src/standalone/email-landing.test.tsx` (16 — flag read and strip, guest + empty only, the notice, Connect opens the picker), `product-events.server.test.ts` (both new counters); `roadmap.open.test.ts` also follows each email's rendered button into the loader, so the href and the counter cannot drift apart. ✅ Real WebKit, 2026-09-24 (Playwright's own browser, fresh context per case): the live storefront page with this build's widget bundle swapped in, at `?from=email`. iPhone 15 (393×659 viewport): the whole notice sits at 406–571px, no scroll, the Input tab active, the flag stripped with `utm_source` and the hash kept, no sideways overflow, and Connect opens the picker; no flag, or a browser that still holds a record: nothing shows. Desktop 1366×900: 285–390px. ❌ Still owed after deploy: the same check against the deployed bundle, and the first `email_landing_empty` rows. AC14 (2026-10-04): ✅ `email-landing.test.tsx` now 24 (8 AC14: exact lead, address span as copy target, buttons Connect / Copy link / Dismiss, copy inside the press, success event, two fallbacks, stale timer, Dismiss clears it) + `product-events.server.test.ts`; ✅ Chromium at 393×659 on the Pages build: notice bottom 573 at load (601 in the fallback), scrollY 0; ❌ owed after deploy: real WebKit on the live page, AC13's iPhone 15 check repeated.

### US-19 · Sending feedback
As a user, I can send Brad feedback from the widget; it reaches his inbox (Resend) AND the `feedback_submissions` table so it's never silently lost; bots are honeypotted; 3/hour/IP.
- Third channel (2026-09-03, US-32 AC9/AC23): a user whose assistant is connected can ask it to report a bug, and the hosted connector files a PUBLIC GitHub issue on this repo (`[connector]` title, `from-connector` label) under a server-held token — no health values, no identifier, capped 20/hour server-wide and 3/day per connection. The product-health loop reads that label weekly beside the inbox and the table (charter Gather, since 2026-09-06); as of 2026-W36 the only such issue is Brad's own path test (#53). 2026-W38: still only Brad's own (#53, and #78 — the US-36 probe).
- AC1 (2026-09-10, night): the 3/hour/IP budget is spent when the request is accepted, not after the email goes out. The counter sat after `sendFeedbackEmail`, so a Resend outage threw past it and handed an attacker unlimited inserts for the length of the outage. It is charged before the work now, so a failing send still costs the caller their attempt. Tests: `app/routes/api.feedback.test.ts`
- AC2 (2026-09-10, night): the honeypot is the schema and only the schema. `website: z.string().max(0)` already rejects a filled field with a 400 before any insert or send, so the "silently succeed" branch below it could never run — it was checking a value Zod had guaranteed empty. The dead branch is gone and the comment says where the trap actually is, so the next reader does not add a second one. Tests: `app/routes/api.feedback.test.ts`
- Evidence: 2026-08 incident — edu app missing `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` sent from the Resend sandbox address; fixed 2026-08-07, DB mirror added so submissions are provable. 2026-W32–W38: zero organic feedback on any channel (emails, table rows, connector issues).
- Tests: 🟡 sendFeedbackEmail + route helpers tested; mirror insert untested (accepted — verified live).

### US-20 · Mobile
As a mobile user, I get a tabbed layout (input/plan/chat) with CSS scroll-snap swiping, and every layout works in real iOS WebKit (not just Chrome emulation).
- Evidence: mobile engagement is much shallower than desktop (2.6 vs 7.4+ min new-user averages) — worth watching in funnel data.
- AC1 (2026-09-07): The deployment WebKit check fails on a missing seeded matrix, missing values, collapsed/content-box cells or page overflow, on both desktop and iPhone.
- AC2 (added by sentry-fix 2026-09-24, Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-6Q): crossing the mobile breakpoint mid-session never crashes the widget. The tab Swiper renders only under `isMobile` (a live `matchMedia` listener at 768px), so a desktop window widened past it unmounts the Swiper and swiper/react destroys the instance, which deletes its params; `swiperRef` still pointed at it, and the next edit ran the autoHeight effect, which threw `undefined is not an object (evaluating 't.params.slidesPerView')` inside a React commit, so the ErrorBoundary replaced the whole widget with "Something went wrong" (three events, Safari 18.7 / Mac, 09-19 to 09-23). `updateAutoHeight` is the one reached method that dereferences the deleted params; `slideTo` checks `destroyed` itself and returns. All three call sites (the tab sync, the autoHeight re-measure, the chat hand-off to slide 0) now go through one `liveSwiper()` accessor that returns null once the instance is destroyed. Stated residual: the ref is never nulled, so a flip back to mobile mounts a fresh instance through `onSwiper` in the layout phase, before any passive effect runs, and the stale one is dropped; `HistoryPanel` nulls its own chart ref in cleanup and has no equivalent hole. Tests: `widget-src/src/components/HealthTool.swiper.test.tsx` (2: a Swiper unmounted by the layout switch is `destroyed` and throws the same TypeError on the real library in jsdom; the ref is read on exactly two lines of HealthTool, the accessor and the `onSwiper` assignment, so a direct call, `?.`, `!.` or an alias anywhere else fails the suite).
- Tests: ✅ `tools/webkit-verify.test.ts` pins failure conditions; the current v2 seeded probe passed live on desktop and iPhone on 2026-09-07. Full journeys still need live verification.

### US-21 · Additional blood tests on the main matrix — **phase 1 SHIPPED 2026-08-07, phase 2 SHIPPED 2026-08-14, phase 3 conversion BUILT 2026-09-18 and SHIPPED 2026-09-23 (factor table verified by Brad before the deploy; recorded 2026-09-27, product-health W39 review R1); the matrix correction UI BUILT 2026-09-24; plausibility bounds still open (heading corrected 2026-09-06; it still read "DRAFT")**
As a user whose lab reports contain tests beyond the core 8 (sodium, GGT, TSH, ferritin…), I can see those stored values as grouped rows under the core matrix and add a test I want to monitor via a "+" button — one place to watch everything.

**What already exists** (why this is surfacing, not building, for phase 1): the `labValues` collection in `health-roadmap.json` already captures every extracted additional test (FHIR `status`/`source`, value+unit as reported, reference ranges, dedup on `(metric_name, recorded_at)`). Today it's visible only in History and at upload-review time (`ReviewTable`); the main matrix never shows it. Label mapping exists (`lib/lab-value-labels.ts`), reference hints exist (`reference-hints.ts`).

**Draft acceptance criteria** (to iterate before build):
- AC1: Stored lab values render as rows beneath the core 8, grouped by panel (Liver · Kidney/renal · Electrolytes · Blood count · Hormones · Lipids-extended · Other), matching the matrix's column/date alignment and scroll-sync.
- AC2: "+ Add test" lets the user pick from a curated catalogue (searchable; canonical unit + validation range per test) or add a free-form test.
- AC3: A known test's row always displays ONE unit; a value arriving in a different unit is converted or flagged at review — never silently mixed into the row (a U/L value charted next to a µkat/L value would be clinically wrong).
- AC4: Upload-extracted and manually-entered values for the same test land in the same row (alias map keyed on stable metric keys — never dedup on raw LLM text, per the documented gotcha).
- AC5: Corrections work exactly like core rows (entered-in-error + correctsId chain — the labValue shape already carries status/source). Built 2026-09-24: a lab cell opens the core matrix's editor, and the correction goes through `record-edits.correctValue` in the unit the value is stored in. Closing a group never drops a correction left open in it: the correction saves, and one the record refuses is still open, with its reason, when the group reopens (Codex, 2026-09-24: closing the group unmounted the editor before its click-away save ran). Tests: `AdditionalLabRows.test.tsx` (a phone's tap, a refusal kept), `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx` (over a real store).
- AC6: Cross-device merge semantics reviewed for the new slot behavior (Fable-level judgment — merge.ts territory).
- **Usage signal (Lane B mandatory): new product event `lab_row_added` for manual adds; phase-1 surfacing measured via a `lab_rows_viewed` event (count metadata). Uploads already emit `upload_saved`.**

**Decisions (Brad, 2026-08-07):**
1. **Units: canonical-units catalogue — option (a).** A new `packages/health-core/src/lab-catalog.ts` defines each test's canonical unit (units.ts discipline; conversion factors added per-metric as implemented, each verifiable). Per-lab reference ranges keep coming from the uploaded report (`referenceLow/High` already stored) — the catalogue carries units/grouping/aliases, not clinical thresholds, so the evidence-citation surface stays small.
2. **Initial panel scope:** Renal (incl. electrolytes) · Liver · Thyroid (TFTs) · Hormones · Vitamins & minerals · Inflammation markers. Grow from there.
3. **Collapsed by default** under the core 8.
4. **Each group renders an icon to the left of its heading** (kidney → Renal, etc.) — inline SVGs, same no-asset-fetch pattern as the backend-picker logos.

**Remaining open questions:** exact test list per panel (Brad to review the catalogue skeleton); mixed-unit handling for values stored before a metric's conversion exists (proposal: display as-reported with the unit label until the conversion lands).

**Phasing** (each phase independently shippable with its own signal):
- **Phase 1 — surface what exists: ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-07.** `AdditionalLabRows` renders grouped, collapsed-by-default read-only rows beneath the blood-test matrix (inline SVG icon per group, count, mixed-unit flag, "Other tests" bucket for uncatalogued names). Logic in `lib/lab-rows.ts` (13 tests); catalogue resolver handles LLM underscore/space variance. Corrections deferred to phase 2 (at the time labValues had no store-level correct method; since 2026-09-03 the store writes one for the upload review's Replace — US-13 AC3 — and the matrix UI for it is what phase 3 still owes). Signal live: `lab_rows_viewed` — 15 events in the first instrumented week (2026-W32) vs 190 `results_viewed` (~8% of viewers expand the additional-lab rows); 2026-W33: 6 (novelty wear-off vs the 08-14 relayout — W34 decides); 2026-W34: 9 (~1.3/day, stabilized — novelty wear-off confirmed, relayout not implicated); 2026-W35: 8 (stable); 2026-W36: 19 (+138% against a down week — plausibly Brad's own connector live-verify sessions loading records; unattributable). **AC1 layout fix 2026-08-14 (found live by Brad):** phase 1 had shipped expanded groups as horizontal "dated value strips", violating AC1's "matching the matrix's column/date alignment" — expanded groups now render the same `bt-*` date-column matrix as the core 8 (shared `BatchDateCell` + CSS vars, per-group date columns, newest column pinned, name column pixel-aligned with the core matrix; layout pinned by a component test + `labGroupMatrix` unit tests). 2026-W37: 19 (Brad's US-36 verify week); 2026-W38: 4 (back to ~1/day organic).
- **Phase 2 — manual add: ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-14 (add only; corrections still open).** "+ Add a blood test" beneath the groups (`AddLabTest.tsx`): catalogue picker grouped by panel, canonical unit FIXED for known tests (AC3), free-form name+unit via "Other", native date input, saves through `bulkSaveLabValues` under the stable catalogue key (AC4 — manual and upload-extracted values share a row), store-side same-day dedup surfaced as an inline notice. Section now renders (with just the button) even before any lab values exist. Gated exactly like the labValues fetch (`isLoggedIn`; always-on for the standalone build). Signal live: `lab_row_added` — 0 events in its first ~1.5 live days (deployed 08-14; true zero, watch W34); 2026-W34: first 3 manual adds ever; 2026-W35: 2; 2026-W36: 2. labValue CORRECTIONS remain open on the website's matrix — they move to phase 3's scope — but since 2026-09-01 they exist on the other writers: `correctValue` (US-31 AC3) corrects a lab row from the CLI and the MCP tools, and the upload review's Replace box (US-13 AC3) writes one for a conflicting upload. Manual adds 2026-W37: 4; 2026-W38: 1.
- **Units fix (same day, found live by Brad):** the catalogue now covers the common FBC/chemistry tests that were dumping into "Other tests" (new `haematology` "Blood count" group — Hb/Hct/RBC/WBC/differential/platelets/MCV/MCH/MCHC/RDW — plus eGFR under renal, globulin under liver), and display units are normalized at render: `normalizeLabUnit` fixes report/LLM spelling ("umol/L"→"µmol/L", "x 10e9/L"→"×10⁹/L", "1.73m2"→"m²"), `displayLabUnit` relabels same-unit spelling variants to the catalogue canonical (haematocrit "ratio" ≡ "L/L" via per-entry `unitAliases`), so false "Units vary between reports" flags are gone. SPELLING ONLY — stored values stay as-reported; genuinely different units still flag mixedUnits (AC3).
- **Adversarial review 2026-08-14 (verdict: ship with fixes — applied same commit):** dedup slots now resolve to the catalogue KEY (an upload's "Gamma GT" + a manual "ggt" on the same day was two active rows — the exact duplicate the notice exists to stop); Greek μ (U+03BC) normalizes to µ (U+00B5); "m^2" joins "m2"→"m²"; `hs_crp` resolves; save() catches store throws; future dates blocked in canSave. **Residual risks accepted:** (1) manual entry has no per-test plausibility bounds — a US-report user can type a g/dL number into the fixed g/L field (10× off, no warning; blast radius is display-only since additional labs feed no suggestion logic; bounds belong to phase 3 with conversion); (2) HistoryPanel still charts raw units/raw-name series (cosmetic inconsistency with the matrix); (3) ~~cross-device merge unions by id, so a same-day name-variant pair created on TWO devices before syncing still survives merge~~ — **closed 2026-09-02 by US-10 AC3**: lab slots are keyed on the catalogue key at merge time (`labSlotKey`), so the variants converge to one active row and the loser flips to `entered-in-error`; (4) eGFR reported as bare "mL/min" deliberately still flags mixed units — raw clearance is genuinely a different quantity than the BSA-normalized canonical.
- **Phase 3 — normalization + corrections: conversion BUILT 2026-09-18, factor table verified by Brad (clinical content), SHIPPED 2026-09-23 (manual deploy 3296cf2; first `lab_unit_refused` 2026-09-24T06:02Z).** Unit conversion at the write for catalogue tests; per-test plausibility bounds for manual entry are still owed. The website matrix's own correction UI (AC5) was built 2026-09-24. The factor table lives in `health_roadmap_algorithm.md` §2 and is pinned to `LAB_CONVERSIONS` by a drift test, so a factor cannot change in one place.
- AC7 (the storage contract, one conversion site): a catalogued test is stored in the catalogue's SI unit on EVERY writer — website, CLI, stdio MCP, hosted MCP — because all three write doors (`appendLabValue`, `bulkAppendValues`, the lab branch of `correctValue`) call one `canonicalLabValue` in `lab-catalog.ts`. A writer sends the number and unit as the lab PRINTED them; nothing upstream converts. A unit spelling the catalogue does not know for that test is REFUSED (`unknown-unit`), and the refusal names the canonical unit and every spelling that test accepts — never guessed from the number, which is the mistake that stores a US lipid as an SI one. A test the catalogue does not know has no SI definition, so it is stored exactly as reported, with its printed unit; refusing it would throw the value away. Prolactin in ng/mL is refused on purpose: the mIU/L factor is assay-dependent. So is urea in mg/dL under the bare name "urea" — a US report prints the nitrogen and the rest of the world the molecule, so mg/dL reaches that slot only under a nitrogen name ("BUN", ×0.357), which `resolveLabCatalogEntry` supplies by returning the entry the PRINTED name earns.
- AC8 (the range travels with the value): `referenceLow` and `referenceHigh` convert by the SAME factor as the value, on every one of those doors. A converted value beside an unconverted range would read as out of range for a result that never was.
- AC9 (rows written before phase 3): corrected at load, never mutated. `migrateFile` appends a converted row with the deterministic id `<id>#si`, `correctsId` pointing at the printed row, the original `createdAt`/`recordedAt`, and flips the printed row to `entered-in-error` (monotonic, so a client that has not converted still converges). Same id and same content on every device, so `mergeFiles` folds the copies into one row — no `#dup-` ids, exactly one active row per slot — and a second migrate is a no-op. NO `schemaVersion` bump: a bump makes every older app and CLI refuse the file (`SchemaTooNewError`), and a converted row is readable by any version. A row already holding the right number (an accepted same-scale spelling, factor 1) is left alone — a correction row there would assert a value was wrong when it never was. Such a row therefore keeps the spelling its report printed ("ug/L" stays "ug/L", not µg/L): it is numerically identical, the catalogue reads it as the canonical unit anywhere it matters, and rewriting a label is not worth a correction row.
- AC10 (`correct_value` converts): the lab branch takes a `unit` and converts it, as the core-metric branch already did; omitting it means `newValue` is already in the unit the row is STORED in. A unit on an uncatalogued test is refused rather than silently ignored — there is nothing to convert it to.
- AC11 (a refused row is shown, not dropped): `bulkAppendValues` returns the refused lab rows with the reason; the website's upload done panel shows them as refused with the printed unit, and the import commit says so in words and offers the units it takes. **Usage signal (Lane B mandatory): `lab_unit_refused` with `{key, unit}` — the catalogue key and the unit spelling only, NEVER the value, which is the whole point of refusing it. It is the signal that says which spelling to add next.** The hosted MCP already counts `unknown-unit` refusals.
- Usage evidence (product-health, `lab_unit_refused`): W39 8 rows, W40 25, each week ONE visitor (33 all-time). 10 of W40's 25 key+unit pairs are spellings of units the catalogue converts (`gm/dL`, `Units/L`, `10^3/cmm`, `10^6/cmm`); the rest are differentials in `cells/uL` and `%`, TSH in `mInt-unit(s)/mL`, FT4 as a `T7 index`, prolactin `ng/mL` and urea `mg/dL` (the last two refused by design).
- AC13 (2026-09-22 feedback: "impossible to edit a mistyped 'add a blood test'"): a mistyped value is fixed where it shows, corrected in place (AC5). Adding a test that already has a value that day no longer dead-ends: the form names the value there and offers "Replace 45 µg/L with 54", a correction through the cell editor's own door (`correctsId`, the same day, `manual_correction`), never a second row. It is not offered when the number typed is the number there, or for an "Other" test typed in another unit. Any change to the form withdraws the offer (the test, its name, its unit, the day or the number), and Replace checks again, just before it writes, that the value it names is still this test on this day in this unit; a refusal says why, in the cell editor's words. A test added under a mistyped NAME stays in the record: the record is append-only, and a correction changes a value, never its test. Signal: `correction_made` counts a Replace as it counts a cell correction. Tests: `AdditionalLabRows.test.tsx`, `AddLabTest.test.tsx`, `HealthTool.journey.test.tsx`.
- AC14 (2026-10-04, Brad; product-health W40 `lab_unit_refused`): `normalizeLabUnit` folds three spellings that name the same unit, for every test. `gm/dL` becomes `g/dL` (the gm fold is held to that one spelling: a bare `gm/L` would land on `g/L`, which the count tests read as giga per litre, and `mg/gm` stays as it was). `Unit` or `Units` becomes `U` (Units/L is U/L). `cmm`, `cu mm` and `c.mm` become `µL` (a cubic millimetre is a microlitre, so 10^3/cmm is ×10³/µL, which is ×10⁹/L, and 10^6/cmm is ×10¹²/L). No factor changes and no new conversion: each fold lands on a spelling the catalogue already accepts or refuses, so a test that refused the canonical spelling still refuses the folded one. Two effects follow. A stored row written before phase 3 in `gm/dL` (haemoglobin, albumin, total protein, globulin, MCHC) is converted on the next load by the existing AC9 path, an `<id>#si` correction at ×10 with its bounds and the old row entered-in-error; `Units/L` and `cmm` rows are factor 1 and left alone. An uncatalogued test's unit is folded where it is shown (an uncatalogued "Units/L" displays as `U/L`); the website's record keeps the printed unit, while a connector import, which files the displayed unit (`mcp-tools.ts`), stores `U/L`. The same unit either way. `Unit(s)` with brackets is not folded. Not folded, proposal only and Brad's call: differentials in `cells/µL` (a ×0.001 factor to ×10⁹/L), differentials in `%` (a different quantity), TSH in `mInt-unit(s)/mL` (an ambiguous prefix), FT4 as a `T7 index` (a different test). Signal: `lab_unit_refused` (AC11) stops showing the W40 pairs that now resolve; it records the printed unit, so a folded spelling that still lands on a refused one (a `cells/cmm` differential, TSH in `Units/L`) keeps appearing, as it should.
- AC15 (2026-10-04, Brad): three more of W40's refused spellings. (1) White cells, the five differentials and platelets accept a count printed in cells per microlitre (`cells/µL`, and `cells/cmm` / `cells/cumm` through the folds) at ×0.001 to ×10⁹/L: a microlitre is 10⁻⁶ L, so 2,400 cells/µL is 2.4 ×10⁹/L. Guard: cells are counted, so a `cells/µL` value, or a reference bound printed with it, that is not a whole number refuses the row, never rescaled; a decimal means the lab printed thousands under the wrong label, and a value and range that disagree leave neither trustworthy. The same reasoning covers a whole number in the wrong magnitude: under `cells/µL`, when both bounds are printed, a value above a hundred times the high bound refuses the row (platelets 250000 with a printed 150–400), and for WBC and platelets only, a value below a hundredth of the low bound does too (a ×10³/µL platelet value of 250 mislabelled against 150000–450000). The differentials get no low-side check, because real counts sit near zero (an eosinophil count of 0, severe neutropenia), and 0 is always accepted (it reads the same on any scale). That is a check of the value against its own printed range, not a clinical threshold (proposed by the orchestrator after the final review; kept, Brad 2026-10-06: a refusal stores nothing wrong, and its one cost is a genuine platelet count near 1,000/µL, which the person can type in ×10⁹/L); real extremes (a WBC of 200,000 or 100 cells/µL against 4,000–11,000) pass. A refusal for the number says what was wrong: a decimal count, or a value and printed range on different scales. The assistant's copy of either says never to re-send it in another unit on its own but to ask the person; the person's copy of the decimal case may point them to the ×10³/µL line on their report. A correction (`correct_value`) runs the same check against the row's existing range, read on the canonical scale, including a legacy row still stored in a factor-1 spelling such as `K/uL` or `10^3/cmm`. The website counts `lab_unit_refused` only for a spelling it does not take; a refusal for the number is not that signal. Every door checks the same row, the connector's import preview included, so a preview never offers what the commit refuses. No per-test minimum yet: Brad sets those (US-21 plausibility bounds, still open), and until then a mislabelled whole number can still land. (2) `cumm` folds to `µL` like `cmm` (a cubic millimetre either way), the common Indian spelling (`10^3/cumm`). (3) A differential printed as `%` stays refused (a share of the white count, not a count, and never multiplied out from the WBC), but its refusal says the absolute count is what the record keeps, so the person looks for the cells/µL or ×10⁹/L line on the same report. The assistant's copy of that refusal also says not to work the count out from the percentage. TSH in `mInt-unit(s)/mL` stays refused (Brad, 2026-10-04: the prefix is ambiguous, milli or a misread micro, and the catalogue never guesses from the value). Three-file sync: the `cells/µL` factor joins the §2 conversion table in `health_roadmap_algorithm.md`; `evidence.ts` and `roadmap_text.html` carry no unit spellings or thresholds that change.
- AC12 (known and not fixed here): `calcium_corrected` carries a plain "calcium" alias, so a plain serum calcium lands on the corrected-calcium row and converts on its factor — the same number, a different test. Pre-existing since the phase-1 catalogue; named here so the next reader does not mistake it for a phase-3 regression.
- Tests: ✅ 2026-09-18 — `lab-catalog.test.ts` (every factor, refusals, spelling folds), `record-edits.test.ts` (all three write doors, reference bounds, uncatalogued as reported), `migrate.test.ts` (`#si` corrections, idempotence, merge convergence), `mcp-tools.test.ts` (the contract text and the refusal answer), `lab-conversions-doc.test.ts` (the §2 table is the code table, both ways). AC14 (2026-10-04): ✅ `lab-catalog.test.ts` now 81 (the W40 pairs resolve, every folded spelling gets exactly its canonical spelling's result across the catalogue, the deferred spellings plus gm/L and mg/gm stay refused, 30+ other units untouched), `migrate.test.ts` now 22 (a legacy gm/dL haemoglobin row converts once at ×10 on load; Units/L and 10^3/cmm rows left alone). AC15 (2026-10-04; merged 2026-10-06, Codex waived by Brad: no Codex runs from cloud sessions; four fresh Opus 5.5 adversary rounds, the last with no blocking findings): ✅ lab-catalog, record-edits, migrate, mcp-tools (both import previews check the printed range, so a preview never offers what the commit refuses; a refused replacement is not counted), lab-conversions-doc, UploadModal.session and AddLabTest (the person sees the canonical unit and the note, never the assistant's copy); each fix proven by reverting it and watching its test fail.

### US-23 · Typed-email reminders — **THE TRUNK (Brad-approved 2026-08-14; LIVE since the 08-14 deploy — heading corrected 2026-09-06, it still said "awaiting deploy")**
As someone who typed my email to get my plan PDF, I get reminded when my check-ups and blood tests come due — because my lane is ~10× the cloud lane (874+ captures, ~4–5/day vs 3 cloud connects ever). **This was the highest-leverage unbuilt thing in the product; it is live, and the ~10:1 bet has held every week since (US-17 evidence).**

**Why it's safe now:** US-22's delivery-validation machine is live and verified (signature-checked bounce/complaint webhook → Klaviyo suppression + `deleteByEmail`). Delivery is the consent gate Brad chose over confirm-clicks; a bounce un-enrols before any reminder could ever fire.

- AC1: When the capture button is pressed, the client sends the **schedule alongside the email** — the same client-computed labels + due dates as US-17, stored as one `reminder_optin_v2` row with `provider: 'typed'`. Nothing else crosses (constitution: calendar, not chart).
- AC2: No reminder is ever sent to a typed address until the plan-ready email has had time to bounce. **Precisely (2026-08-14, second review):** the gate is a quiet period, not positive delivery confirmation — the cron skips typed rows for 3 calendar days from `created_at` (effective floor ~2d8h given the 08:00 UTC send hour; never `updated_at`, which refreshes touch), and the bounce/complaint webhook un-enrols within minutes-to-hours, comfortably inside it. This covers the overdue-at-capture case too. Residual, accepted: a plan-ready send that FAILS outright (Resend outage) is logged, not retried — the enrolment stands on the typed consent + quiet period alone. Upgrading to a Resend `delivered`-event flag is future hardening.
- AC3 (both lanes): Every reminder email carries the user's **full upcoming schedule**, not just the due item — the email is the surviving artifact for a user whose localStorage is long gone (constitution: durability). The server already stores the whole list; this costs nothing.
- AC4: Labels are **specific** ("Colonoscopy, due May 2027"), per the constitution's recall-letter decision — and **server-validated against health-core's `SCHEDULE_LABELS` allow-list**: an unauthenticated capture names someone else's inbox, so no client-authored text may ever transit into an email (the old free-text `label` field was the hole). The capture UI carries a one-line disclosure that typing the email turns reminders on, with the off switch named.
- AC5: EVERY reminder to a typed address carries the one-click unsubscribe prominently in the body, not just the header (a superset of US-17 AC2c's "first reminder" — a delivered-but-mistyped address belongs to a stranger; rare sends + one click = bounded harm).
- AC6 (both lanes): **The annual floor** — if the computed schedule has nothing due within 12 months, seed one "Annual health check-in" item 12 months out. Every enrolled person gets at least one touch a year; a colonoscopy-every-10-years user must not get a decade of silence (Brad, 2026-08-14).
- AC7: Re-capturing with the same email **refreshes the schedule ONLY** — the token and `last_sent` cooldowns are preserved, and a typed write can never touch a provider-verified row. Each of those three is load-bearing: a rotated token breaks the unsubscribe links in every email already sent (the page would claim success while the row lives on); reset cooldowns turn one re-capture/day into daily re-sends of whatever is due; and clobbering a verified row would 404 the victim's own device into a silent "you unsubscribed".
- AC8: Abuse bound for `victim@example.com` (rebuilt after the second adversarial review found the first version had three bypasses): **the plan-ready email fires ONLY on a NEW enrolment** — a schedule-less body sends nothing (was: per-capture), a capture against a cloud-enrolled address sends nothing (was: per-capture at exactly the most engaged users), and **unsubscribe is a durable TOMBSTONE for typed rows** (schedule emptied, row + token kept), so a replayed capture is a refresh (no email), not a fresh enrolment (was: unsubscribe→re-capture minted a new email each cycle). Plus: per-IP rate limit (20/hr) beside the per-email 5/day, the response is CONSTANT (an `enrolled` field was a membership oracle for typed emails), labels allow-listed (AC4), unsubscribe links never rot (AC7 + provider-upgrade token preservation), complaint (US-22 AC10) suppresses + un-enrols immediately.
- AC9: Shopify surface only — the capture UI is now gated on `SHOPIFY_SURFACE` **explicitly** (before 2026-08-14 Pages was protected only by its hardcoded `data-logged-in="true"` attribute — an accident, not a gate).
- AC10 (2026-09-24): every reminder email, every provider, opens the tool through `${APP_BASE_URL}/roadmap/open?src=reminder`, never the GitHub Pages build. Its clicks count as `reminder_email_clicked` (server-only, no metadata), apart from the plan-ready email's `report_email_clicked`; the query only picks the counter, by exact equality, and the destination stays one literal (US-22 AC13). The footer makes no storage claim that is false for a lane: "Dr Brad's server keeps your reminder calendar and this email address. Your plan is not stored there." replaced "Your health data lives only in your own cloud storage", false for the typed lane (169 of 183 rows on 2026-09-24). The button reads "Open the Health Roadmap", and the email has no em dashes. Why: the button linked `drbradstanfield.github.io/roadmap/`, a different site from the storefront where every reminder reader made their plan, and a 404 on the day it was found; five reminders had gone out, one since 09-18. `PAGES_APP_URL` went with it: the button was its only caller. The unsubscribe page both emails link to said the same false thing ("it lives only in your own cloud storage") and now says only that the health data is unaffected (adversarial review, 2026-09-25). Tests: `plan-ready-email.test.ts` (the button, the footer, no em dash), `roadmap.open.test.ts` (the counter), `product-events.server.test.ts` (server-only), `reminders-v2.unsubscribe.test.ts` (the confirm page).
- AC11 (Brad, 2026-09-28): the capture box and the reminders opt-in name the MicroVitamin mailing list and say whose it is. The box reads "Getting your plan adds you to the **MicroVitamin mailing list** (Dr Brad's supplement company), and we'll email you when a check-up or blood test is due. We store only check-up names and dates, never your results. Every email has a one-click unsubscribe." The reminders control's unticked box reads "Also add me to the MicroVitamin mailing list (Dr Brad’s supplement company). Unsubscribe anytime." Why: every sign-up from both stores joins MicroVitamin's list (one Klaviyo account, on purpose), and the server records marketing consent (`email.marketing.consent: 'SUBSCRIBED'` in `klaviyo.server.ts`). The old copy named only the reminders, and the opt-in promised "Dr Brad’s evidence-based health emails", so neither said what the person was joining. Decided (Brad, 2026-09-28: "stay as is"): the capture box discloses rather than asks. The stricter EU/UK reading of GDPR consent, a separate unticked box for the list unbundled from getting the plan, was offered and not chosen.
- **Usage signal:** `reminder_optin` vs `reminder_optout`, both carrying `provider` so the lanes are judged separately; same ~30% kill criterion as US-17. Both sides of the typed ratio are counted SERVER-side: optin on the capture route (only NEW enrolments — refreshes don't inflate the denominator, and abuse enrolments are counted rather than invisible), optout on the unsubscribe page (the typed lane's primary off switch — it was structurally blind before the first review). Bounce rate on new sends stays the estimator for the legacy-~900 backfill decision (US-22 AC8, still Brad's open call).
- The **annual floor (AC6) is computed client-side AND clamped server-side on every write** (`ensureAnnualFloor` — pure date arithmetic, so the server stays a dumb scheduler). The server clamp is an integrity bound, not UX: anyone who knows a typed user's email can overwrite their schedule via re-capture (AC7's refresh is unauthenticated by construction), which would otherwise be a silent per-user kill switch for the retention engine; with the clamp, the worst an attacker achieves is degrading a victim to annual check-ins — each carrying the one-click off switch. Tombstones (explicit unsubscribe) are exempt: an off stays off. `annual_checkin` is a first-class `ReminderCategory` (group `annual`, 365-day cooldown).
- **Known residual risks, accepted knowingly (second review, 2026-08-14):** (1) a typed schedule remains rewritable by anyone who knows the email — bounded by the floor clamp, label allow-list and real-date validation to "someone could change WHICH canonical items/dates a victim is reminded of", with every email carrying the off switch; (2) a deliberate re-capture refills a tombstoned schedule — that's the opt-out model's re-consent path, and an attacker using it buys only rare reminder-cadence emails; (3) the rate limiters are per-process in-memory (reset on deploy, per-machine) — bounds are soft, ×2 across the two Fly apps.
- Evidence: first live week (2026-W33, deployed 08-14): 17 typed enrolments vs 3 cloud rows all-time — the ~10:1 typed-lane bet held almost exactly; plan-ready CTR 40%. Week 2 (2026-W34): 35 typed enrolments (accelerating), plan-ready click:send ratio 60% (21 click events / 35 sends — event-level, no per-recipient dimension yet), 0 bounces, 0 complaints. Week 3 (2026-W35): 31 enrolments (typed-dominant; moved with the week's -26% reach dip, not worse); 29 sends / 10 click events (34% — uncohorted drift, not a verdict); 0 bounces/complaints in W34–W35 (all-time: 1 bounce, W33; 0 complaints). Production rows with provider 'typed' + delivered emails now exist, though the deliberate end-to-end capture walk-through the Tests line calls for is still owed. Week 4 (2026-W36): 20 enrolments (moved with the reach dip), 19 sends / 11 click events, 0 bounces/complaints; typed rows 101 of 109 all-time — and the typed lane produced the first two observable reminder sends (08-31, 09-01; see US-17) and the first opt-out (1/109). Weeks 5–6 (2026-W37/W38): 26 and 14 enrolments; sends / click events 24/14 and 13/8; 0 bounces/complaints; typed rows 136 of 149. Week 7 (2026-W39): 47 enrolments; sends / click events 47/26; 1 bounce (second ever); typed rows 178 of 195. US-22 AC13 / US-23 AC10 live 09-24: `email_landing_empty` fired twice in its first days (one visitor, 09-24, 15 min apart) and that visitor fired nothing else — no recovery yet (W39 backlog #4); `reminder_email_clicked` 0 (two of the week's three sends predate the deploy; one after — a zero at n=1).
- Tests: ✅ 25 server/health-core cases 2026-08-14, incl. a ROUTE-level suite (`api.measurements.test.ts`) pinning the isNew gate, the schedule-less and cloud-row no-send paths, the constant response, the tombstone, `upsertOptin`'s typed-refusal, the server floor clamp, and the real-date refine. ❌ live verify after deploy: a real typed capture → row lands with provider 'typed' → plan-ready email carries the calendar. ✅ AC11 (2026-09-28): `ResultsPanel.local-first.test.tsx` pins the capture box's list sentence; `standalone/reminders-control.test.tsx` pins the opt-in's label, placeholder and aria-label. ✅ Real WebKit on the live page, 2026-09-28 (edu-93, iPhone 13 and desktop), AC11: the capture box showed the new sentence, with "MicroVitamin mailing list" at weight 600. The reminders opt-in's label is pinned by reminders-control.test.tsx. Its live check is owed, because it needs a connected cloud.

### US-24 · Add my check-ups to my calendar — **Brad-approved 2026-08-14 (links, not attachments)**
As a user reading my plan or a reminder email, I can add any due item to my own calendar in one tap — Google/Apple's calendar becomes a reminder engine Brad never operates (the zero-server rung of the reminders ladder).
- AC1: Implemented as **Google Calendar template links** (`calendar.google.com/calendar/render?action=TEMPLATE…`) — plain URLs, no attachments (Brad, 2026-08-14: cleaner than .ics; Resend supports attachments but links beat them on deliverability and simplicity).
- AC2: Each link creates an all-day event on the due date, titled with the label, description linking back to the tool — **via the canonical storefront URL** (`drstanfield.com/pages/roadmap`), never a fly.dev backend host: the description is visible text in the saved event, and a raw redirect URL reads as phishing (found live by Brad, fixed 2026-08-14; cost accepted — calendar-sourced returns go uncounted, only the email buttons route through `/roadmap/open`, since 2026-09-24 both of them, US-23 AC10).
- AC3: Links appear wherever the schedule renders in email (plan-ready email once US-23 gives it a schedule; every reminder email).
- AC4: Zero server-side storage change; zero new data crosses.
- Tests: ✅ URL-building pinned (`plan-ready-email.test.ts`: TEMPLATE params, exclusive end date incl. year rollover, re-entry pointer); email placement pinned in the calendar-section suites.

### US-25 · The mobile app — architectural destination (stub; this-year ambition once the web loop is proven)
As the avatar's future self, I have an app that makes the whole thing automatic: **on-device notifications** (reminders with no server at all — the true serverless end-state), **HealthKit ingestion** (US labs push results into Apple Health; the app reads bloods, recomputes the plan, no typing), durable storage (no localStorage wipe anxiety). The enrolment/consent model built for US-17/US-23 transfers as-is; the server calendar shrinks toward zero as users migrate. Every near-term decision gets checked against the constitution's question: *does this move toward or away from the phone?*

### US-26 · Action links — every reminder pairs the "what" with a "where" (stub)
As a user told a lipid panel is due, the reminder also tells me where to get one — direct-to-consumer lab links (Quest/Ulta-style, US), supplement refills via Brad's store, later pharmacy integration. Start affiliate-grade (plain links, zero API work); the click-through data is the evidence needed before building any real integration. This is the monetization seam, honestly labeled — and it must never gate the reminder itself.

## Epic G — Operational bots (non-avatar: Brad is the user; ops-safety specs only)

### US-27 · YouTube bot posting caps hold when the data layer fails — **added by sentry-fix 2026-08-27 (spec hole found via Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-64/65/66)**
As Brad, on whose channel the bot posts publicly, its safety caps (50 replies/day, 10/video lifetime) bind under ALL conditions — including when the Supabase reads behind them fail.
- AC1: A failed cap read (today's post count, per-video totals) fails distinguishably (throws, same convention as the tick's other pre-posting reads) — never a value indistinguishable from zero posts.
- AC2: With either cap unknown, the tick posts nothing (top-level or follow-up), logs the skip, and retries next tick.
- Tests: 🟡 `youtube-bot.server.test.ts` pins AC1 at the `readPostingCaps` gate (Supabase error on either read → rejects; success → both caps). AC2's tick half (catch → post nothing → log → retry) is review-verified only — `tick()` isn't exported; extracting it is a future testability task.
- Origin: from 2026-08-26 an intermittent Supabase-side PGRST303 ("JWT issued at future") made ~⅓ of cap reads error; each affected tick silently re-armed the full daily budget (error was mapped to count 0 / empty map).
- Status: shipped by sentry-fix's Tier 3 PR #30, deployed 2026-08-27; the PGRST303 class (JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-64/65/67) has been quiet since 2026-08-28T16:11Z (product-health W35, W36). Brad closed decision issue #32 as leave-to-Supabase.

### US-28 · Daily crons survive transient lock errors — **added by sentry-fix 2026-08-28 (spec hole found via Sentry JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-66/68)**
As Brad, whose server runs five daily jobs behind a cross-machine day lock (reminder emails, trending ×2, the YouTube ops summary, the chat-text purge), a transient database error while acquiring the lock delays that machine's attempt to the next hourly tick — it never silently cancels the job for the day.
- AC1: A transient DB error in `tryAcquireCronLock` (lock UPDATE or verify SELECT) fails distinguishably (throws, US-27 AC1's convention) — never the same `false` that means "another machine won". Permanent conditions (no admin client, missing seed row, malformed date) still return `false`: retrying cannot fix them.
- AC2: On such an error the caller leaves the day unmarked and retries next tick; a genuine `false` still ends its day. Because the day lock itself is the cross-machine gate, a retry can never double-run a job another machine already claimed.
- Tests: 🟡 `cron-lock.test.ts` pins AC1 (update error → rejects; verify error → rejects; won/lost/missing-seed/bad-date → boolean). `reminder-v2-cron.server.test.ts` pins AC2 for the reminder caller (rejection → re-attempt next tick then run; false → no re-attempt). The other two callers (trending, youtube-bot-summary) inherit AC2 through their existing tick catch — review-verified only (identical tick shape; exercising each would repeat the reminder test around modules that self-start on import — trending's starter is disabled under NODE_ENV=test, and the summary cron's missing test guard is flagged follow-up drift).
- Origin: the US-27 platform fault also hit `tryAcquireCronLock` (JAVASCRIPT-REMIX-66: 4 events 08-26/27; -68: reminder_v2_cron + trending_cron 08-28). Every caller mapped the error's `false` to "day done", so one transient error made that machine skip its daily job; whether the job ran at all then depended on the other machine winning the lock.
- **The chat digest is deleted (Brad, 2026-09-10).** One of the two ops summaries mailed every chat turn and reply of the past day to Brad — message BODIES, found by an audit of what a skeptical assistant reads out of this repo. Gone with it: the module, its admin trigger, its SIGTERM stop, its cron-lock name and its seed row; nothing imported it but itself. Brad: "I don't have time to read the responses anyway." The count stays five because `chat_text_purge` (US-15 AC7) took its place in the lock table, and the AC1/AC2 behaviour above is unchanged.
- Status: shipped by sentry-fix's Tier 3 PR #35, deployed 2026-08-28 — the day the first genuine reminder send came due inside the fault window (US-17). REMIX-66/68 quiet since; -68 marked resolved.
